    - **Tests:** host tests for the ext2 and FAT16 mapping through `CoreDriver` (`ext2 attributes reach stat and directory entries through the core driver`, the extended `stat reports type, size, ...`), 200-byte names and paths near `PATH_MAX` on ext2 and tmpfs through the VFS, the virtual filesystems' modes and mount-point nodes, `PATH_MAX`/`NAME_MAX` limits in `path_tests.cpp`, `spawn_request_tests.cpp` and the core's `ext2_image_tests.cpp`. `libctest` checks `ENAMETOOLONG` at `PATH_MAX` (and `PATH_MAX - 1` fitting), a relative path too long once resolved, a `NAME_MAX` name in `/tmp` and `d_ino` against `st_ino`. The shell test's `ext2` part lists `/mnt` with the 73-character name whole, checks `ls -l` lines for modes, owners, sizes and times, `cat`s the long-named file, and lists an 80-character name in `/tmp`. Step 1 passed `./build.sh unit-test` (867 cases), `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`, the full matrix (38 cases, including the Release shell case) and `BUILD_TYPE=Release ./build.sh --arch x86_64 test`.
  - **Step 2 done: symbolic links, resolved in the VFS walk.**
    - **The walk:** `VFS::ResolvePath(path, Follow, PathWalk*)` (`src/kernel/filesystem/path_walk.cpp`, declared in `vfs.h`) turns an absolute path into its physical form, without links, `.`, `..` or repeated `/`, which every other VFS call and every driver takes; no driver follows a link. It keeps the physical prefix resolved so far and the rest still to walk (right-aligned, so a link's target goes in front of it without moving it) in one 3 KiB heap `PathWalk` per walk, counts expansions instead of recursing, and fails with `WalkResult::Loop` past `User::MaximumSymbolicLinks` (40, Linux's `MAXSYMLINKS`). An absolute target restarts at `/`, a relative one goes on from the link's directory. `..` drops the last component of the physical prefix, so after a link it leaves the target's directory (POSIX's physical walk) and from a mount's root it reaches the mount point's parent; it needs an existing directory (`NotFound`/`NotDirectory`), while other missing components are left for the call to report. `Follow::All` follows a link in the last component (open, stat, exec, chdir, readdir, mount and umount targets, realpath), `Follow::NotLast` does not (lstat, readlink, unlink, rmdir, mkdir, rename, symlink's new name and both of link's names, as Linux), and a trailing `/` always follows. A resolved path, or a link's target together with the rest still to walk, longer than `PATH_MAX` fails with `NameTooLong`.
    - **Finding links:** the components appended since the last check are checked together (at the end, before `..` and before an unfollowed last component) by `VFS::FirstLink`, which asks each mount's driver about its own part of the path through the new `Driver::FindLink`, from the deepest mount up; a mount point is never a link. A path without links therefore costs one extra driver walk per mount it crosses, and drivers that hold no links (`Driver::SupportsLinks`: FAT16 through its core capabilities, `/system`, `/dev`, `/proc`) answer at once. Measured on the host (unsanitized Release build), `Files::Stat` of a five-component `/tmp` path took about 225 ns against about 95 ns for the bare VFS calls it replaced: about 90 ns for the walk and 40 ns for its heap buffer. In QEMU, copying the path from user memory byte by byte through the page tables costs more than either.
    - **Joining and the working directory:** `Path::Resolve`'s textual normalisation is gone. `Path::Join` takes an absolute path as given, or joins a relative one to the working directory, applying only its leading `.` and `..` there (the working directory is physical, so its textual parent is its real one) and leaving the rest to the walk. `Files` walks every path argument under the filesystem lock (a `Resolved` helper in `files.cpp`), so the walk and the operation see the same tree, and `chdir` stores the walk's result, so a working directory never holds a link. `/proc/self` is still rewritten textually after the join; it is not a link.
    - **Drivers:** `Filesystem::Driver` gained `SupportsLinks(LinkKind)`, `FindLink`, `ReadLink` (copies what fits and reports the whole length), `SymbolicLink` and `Link`. `CoreDriver` maps them onto the core's `Lookup`, `GetAttributes`, `ReadLink`, `SymbolicLink`, `Link` and capabilities, so ext2 reads its fast and slow links and will write them once the core does. tmpfs holds symbolic links (`Tmpfs::SymbolicLink`, `ReadLink`, `FindLink`, `IsLinkNode`; the target is the node's data and counts toward the 4 MiB; `S_IFLNK` `0777`) but no hard links, since a tmpfs node holds its own name; that needs a name/node split, left for when it is wanted.
    - **Syscalls:** `LinkStat` (66), `ReadLink` (67), `SymbolicLink` (68), `Link` (69) and `ResolvePath` (70, behind `realpath`), the flag `OpenNoFollow` (`SyscallErrorLoop` on a final link) and `SyscallErrorLoop` (`ELOOP`, 40). `Files::SymbolicLink` and `Files::Link` fail in Linux's order: `EEXIST`, `ENOENT` for a missing parent, `EXDEV` (link only), `EROFS`, then `EPERM` where the filesystem cannot hold that kind of link, as Linux's vfat answers, so FAT16 refuses both with `EPERM`, tmpfs hard links too, and the read-only ext2 mount gives `EROFS`; `link` of a directory is `EPERM`. `unlink` and `rename` act on a link itself, `rename` replaces a link at its target name, and `Spawn` follows links (`SpawnStatus::Loop`).
    - **libc and programs:** `lstat`, `readlink`, `symlink`, `link`, `readlinkat`, `symlinkat`, `linkat` (with `AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW` through `realpath`), `realpath` (a null buffer allocates), `O_NOFOLLOW` (Linux's generic `0400000` on both targets), `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`/`AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW`, `ELOOP` and its `strerror`; `O_CREAT | O_EXCL` checks with `lstat`, so a dangling link counts. `ls` `lstat`s entries and `ls -l` prints `name -> target`; new programs `ln [-s] <target> <link>` and `readlink [-f] <path>`; `cat` prints why it could not open a file.
    - **Tests:** host tests in `tests/unit/kernel/filesystem/symbolic_link_tests.cpp` (absolute and relative targets, chains, a link to a directory mid-path, `..` after a link, `..` before a missing directory or after a file, crossing between `/tmp`, `/etc`, `/` and `/proc`, loops and a chain of exactly 40, a dangling link and `O_CREAT` through it, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `lstat` against `stat`, `ENAMETOOLONG` after expansion, `chdir` through a link, `rm`/`rename`/`rmdir`/`mkdir` on links, the `symlink`/`link`/`readlink` errors and `realpath`), `ext2 links are followed by the VFS walk, into and out of other mounts` in `filesystem_tests.cpp` (the core image's fast link and dangling slow link, `/tmp` links into `/mnt` and back out with `..`, `chdir` through one, `EROFS` on ext2, `EPERM` on FAT16, `EXDEV`), `Path::Join` in `path_tests.cpp` and the syscall dispatch. `libctest` checks `lstat`, `readlink`, `symlink`, `realpath`, `ELOOP`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `O_EXCL` on a link, the `link` and `symlink` errors and `chdir` through a link. The shell test's `ext2` part `cat`s through `fast-link` and `slow-link` and checks `ls -l`'s `-> target` for both, and the new `links` part (`tools/smoke/cases/shell/links.py`) makes a link in `/tmp` with `ln -s`, reads and `readlink`s it, lists it with `ls -l`, sees a loop fail with `Too many levels of symbolic links` and `ln` refuse a hard link with `Operation not permitted`, and removes the link while its target stays. Step 2 passed `./build.sh unit-test` (880 cases, ASan and UBSan), `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"` and the full matrix (38 cases).
  - **Step 3 done: ext2 writes files in the shared core.** The kernel still mounts ext2 read-only (`CoreDriver` asks for it), so nothing changes in QEMU until step 5.
    - **Read-write mounts:** `Ext2Type().Mount` with `read_only = false` gives a writable mount unless the volume must not be written, and then mounts read-only with a host log line saying why (`WriteRefusal` in `ext/ext2.cpp`, `CheckGroups` in `ext/allocation.cpp`): an unknown read-only compatible feature, an ext3 journal (`has_journal`, writes need it, Part 6), a compatible feature outside `dir_prealloc`, `ext_attr`, `resize_inode` and `dir_index` (the ones whose rules the driver keeps), an impossible `s_first_ino`, a volume not cleanly unmounted (`s_state` without the clean bit) or with errors recorded, inconsistent group descriptors, or a device that refuses writes. The mount recomputes the free counts from the descriptors, bumps `s_mnt_count`, sets `s_mtime`, clears the clean bit and writes the superblock and flushes before anything else changes. `Sync` (and so `Unmount`) writes the changed descriptor blocks, flushes, writes the superblock with the clean bit and `s_wtime`, and flushes again; the first change after a `Sync` clears the clean bit on disk first (`BeginChange`), so the volume says clean exactly when it is. Only the primary superblock and descriptor table are written, as Linux's ext2 does: the sparse_super backups hold geometry and features that nothing here changes but `large_file`, which e2fsck leaves out of its comparison, and e2fsck recounts free space whenever it falls back on a backup; `e2fsck -fn` finds nothing to report. Damage found while writing (`Error::Corrupt` from a change) sets the errors bit (`NoteError`), and unless `s_errors` says continue, further changes fail with `ReadOnly`; panic and unknown values count as remount-ro, since the core cannot panic its host.
    - **Allocation (`ext/allocation.cpp`, `common/bitmap.cpp`):** the group descriptor table is read into memory at every mount (`LoadInode` uses it too) and its free counts and the superblock's change with each allocation; bitmaps go through the block cache. Blocks are found from a goal, the block after the file's previous one or the start of its inode's group, first in the goal group from the goal on, then in the following groups; a found block that is metadata of its group (bitmaps, inode table, superblock and descriptor copies with the reserved descriptor blocks) or a count that disagrees with its bitmap is `Corrupt`. New inodes go in the parent's group when it has free inodes and blocks, else the next group that has (Orlov-lite), never below `s_first_ino`, and a free bit whose inode still has links is `Corrupt`. Freeing checks the bit was set. The reserved blocks (`s_r_blocks_count`) are the superuser's, and FeatherOS has no users, so every caller counts as root and may use them.
    - **Operations (`ext/write.cpp`):** `Create` validates the name, walks the directory once to see the name is new and find the first record with room (a used record's slack or an unused record), allocates the inode, writes it zeroed and filled (mode from the request, owner and group 0, times now, one link, `i_extra_isize` 32 on large inodes with the creation time) and then the entry, with the filetype byte when the feature is on; a full directory grows by one block (through an indirect block when it passes 12), written before the directory's size takes it in, and a failure gives the inode back. `Write` maps blocks through a `MapCursor` that holds the indirect blocks on the way and writes a changed one (deeper ones first) when the way leaves it; holes get zero-filled blocks linked only once written, missing indirect blocks are allocated zeroed, and a write starting past the end leaves the gap a hole and zeroes the old last block's tail first. It updates `i_size` (`i_size_high` for regular files), `i_blocks` in 512-byte units, mtime and ctime, sets `large_file` in the superblock before the first inode past 2 GiB (a revision 0 volume is not upgraded, so its files stay below 2 GiB), and returns the bytes written before the volume filled. `Truncate` grows into a hole, or shrinks: the new last block's tail is zeroed, the one indirect tree that reaches over the new end is cut in place (`ShrinkIndirect`, written before what it lost is freed), the trees wholly past it leave the inode, which is written, and are then freed (`FreeTree`, batching one group's bitmap at a time). `Remove` takes files, symbolic links and special nodes (`IsDirectory` for directories, `.` and `..`): the record is merged into the one before it, or marked unused when first in its block, the directory written, then the link count dropped; at zero the inode is written with `i_dtime`, then its blocks (none for a fast link or a device, told apart by `i_blocks` as on Linux) and its extended attribute block (reference count dropped, freed at zero) are freed and the inode bit cleared. A changed directory loses `dir_index`'s hash-index flag before its blocks change, as on Linux. `SetAttributes` stores permission bits, 32-bit owner and group and access and modification times, and sets ctime. Reserved inodes other than the root cannot be changed. Directories, rename and links stay `Unsupported` until step 4.
    - **Write ordering:** metadata is written through: every block goes to the device at once through the cache (`BlockCache::Write`, which keeps cached copies current), and only the descriptors and the superblock's counts wait for `Sync`. A block or inode is marked in its bitmap before anything points to it, new contents before the pointer to them, indirect blocks before the inode, the inode before its directory entry; removal runs the other way. A crash then leaves only what e2fsck repairs in preen mode (leaked blocks and inodes, counts, sizes and `i_blocks` that lag, an unattached inode or a link count one too high), never a pointer to something free or unwritten, and the volume is not clean, so it next mounts read-only. The comment at the top of `write.cpp` states this; only `Sync`'s flush is a barrier, so a device that reorders writes in a volatile cache could still break it on power loss.
    - **Concurrency:** changes and `Sync` must not run at the same time as any other operation on the mount, reads may run together (`include/fs/ext2.h`); the kernel's filesystem lock serialises everything. The core holds no host lock across a device call.
    - **Layout:** the class moved to `ext/ext2_filesystem.h`, shared by `ext2.cpp` (mounting and reading), `allocation.cpp` and `write.cpp`; `superblock.cpp` gained the state fields, `GroupDescriptor`, `EncodeSuperblockState` and `GroupHasSuperblock`, `inode.cpp` `EncodeInode` (read-modify-write of the first 160 bytes, extended times where `i_extra_isize` covers them), `directory.cpp` `RecordLength`, `StoreEntry` and `EntryFileType`.
    - **`fscli`:** `cp-in <image>[:N] <host file> <path>` copies a host file in (into a directory under its own name, replacing a file of that name, with the host file's permission bits; a file it made goes again if the copy fails) and `rm <image>[:N] <path>` removes a file or link; both open the image read-write and fail when the volume mounts read-only. They work on FAT16 too. `MountedVolume::Unmount` reports a failed final sync.
    - **Tests:** CMake finds `e2fsck` beside `mke2fs` (Homebrew keg, then `PATH`) and the tests skip that check without it. `tests/ext2_write_tests.cpp` runs over the `mke2fs` images and requires `e2fsck -fn` to exit 0 after each scenario, with `debugfs -R cat`/`stat` reading contents and modes back: the mount state and its marking around `Sync`; every read-only fallback (with nothing written); create, write and read on 1 KiB, 4 KiB, no-filetype and revision 0 volumes with the name and type errors; writes through direct, single and double indirect blocks with exact block counts; holes and the zeroed tail; shrinking across every indirect boundary with exact free counts, growing into a hole, and truncating the image's double and triple indirect files; removing files, hard links, both kinds of symbolic link, a FIFO and every record of a multi-block directory, then reusing the space; an extended attribute block added with `debugfs ea_set`; a root directory grown past 12 blocks and emptied again; filling the volume to `NoSpace` and then the inodes, freeing and reusing; attributes with nanoseconds on large inodes; `large_file` set by a 3 GiB sparse write and refused on revision 0. A crash test stops the device after each of the 248 writes of a mixed scenario (`MemoryDevice::FailWritesAfter`) and requires that e2fsck reports only the routine repairs above (a deliberately swapped inode and entry write fails it at write 3). A random test runs 1200 and 600 creates, writes, truncates, removes and remounts against an in-memory model on the 1 KiB and 4 KiB images, with e2fsck along the way and a full read-back at the end (3 s with ASan). `common_tests.cpp`, `block_cache_tests.cpp` and `ext2_format_tests.cpp` cover the bitmaps, `BlockCache::Write` and the encoders; `MemoryDevice` keeps its bytes in one allocation so 8 MiB copies stay cheap in unoptimised builds, and the shared image helpers moved to `tests/ext2_image.cpp`. `fs.core:fscli cp-in` (FAT16), `ext2-cp-in` (files over the double indirect range, into directories, replacing, an MBR partition, refusals including the ext3 image) and `ext2-rm` check `fscli` with `e2fsck -fn` and `debugfs`. Step 3 passed `./build.sh unit-test` (904 cases, ASan and UBSan), the standalone core build (114 tests) and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`.
  - **Step 4 done: ext2 directories, rename and links in the shared core.** The kernel still mounts ext2 read-only, so nothing changes in QEMU until step 5.
    - **Operations (`ext/namespace.cpp`, new):** creating and removing moved there from `write.cpp` as `CreateNode` and `RemoveNode`, shared by files, directories and symbolic links. `MakeDirectory` allocates the inode (`AllocateInode` with `bg_used_dirs_count`), one block holding `.` (12 bytes) and `..` (the rest), with the filetype byte when the volume has the feature, and gives the directory two links and its parent one more; `DirectoryGroup` (`allocation.cpp`) puts a directory made in the root in the group with the fewest directories among those with at least the average free inodes and blocks (Orlov-lite, deterministic, ties to the lowest group), and every other one starts in its parent's group, its block in its own group. `RemoveDirectory` takes only `.`, `..` (which must name the parent, else `Corrupt`) and unused records, gives `NotEmpty` otherwise, `InvalidArgument` for `.` and `..` (as FAT16 does) and `Busy` for the root. `Rename` handles a new name in the same or another directory, replaces a file or an empty directory of the same kind (`NotDirectory`/`IsDirectory` for mixed kinds), refuses a directory moved into itself or below it with `InvalidArgument` (`CheckOutside` walks `..` up from the new parent, at most as many steps as the volume has inodes, `Corrupt` past that), and does nothing for two names of one inode; a directory with a new parent has its `..` rewritten and both parents' counts fixed, the renamed inode gets a new ctime and both parents new mtime and ctime. `SymbolicLink` stores a target under 60 bytes in `i_block` with no blocks (fast) and a longer one in one block (slow), mode `0777 | S_IFLNK`, `EXT2_FT_SYMLINK`; targets from `MaximumPathLength` (1024, new in `filesystem.h`) on are `NameTooLong`, empty ones or ones with a NUL `InvalidArgument`. `Link` refuses directories with the new core error `Error::NotPermitted` (EPERM, as Linux; `fscli` prints `operation not permitted`) and gives `TooManyLinks` at 32000 links (`EXT2_LINK_MAX`; `MakeDirectory` and a directory moving to a new parent check the parent the same way). Unlike the plan's "regular files and symbolic links only", special nodes may have hard links too, as on Linux. Directories grow through `AddEntry` (indirect blocks included) and never shrink: removed records join the one before or are marked unused, so every block stays covered by records aligned to 4. Helpers: `SearchDirectory` (the name, or the first record with room), `DropRecord` and `RewriteRecord` (both check the record still names the inode they expect), `UpdateDirectory` (a parent's link count and times, never below 2), `IsEmptyDirectory`, `DropLink` (one link fewer; the last marks the inode deleted, takes a directory's `..` out of its parent's count, then frees blocks and inode) and `CheckReleasable` (in `write.cpp`, the extent and extended-attribute checks `Remove` had).
    - **Write order:** a link count goes up before the entry or new `..` that adds the link is on the disk, and down only after it is gone; a new directory's `..` counts as soon as its inode is in use, so the parent's count is written before the inode, and a removed directory's `..` stops counting once its inode is marked deleted, so the parent's count drops after that. A rename of S from P1 to P2 over a possible T: (1) for a directory S with a new parent, P2's count up; (2) to_name names S, T's record pointed at S in place or a new record added; (3) the old record dropped, found again first since adding may have moved records of the same directory; (4) S's `..` pointed at P2, then P1's count down; (5) S's ctime; (6) T loses a link and is freed with the last, as by unlink or rmdir. Between (2) and (3) S has both names: a file's count is one too low, which e2fsck raises, and a directory's second name e2fsck reports as a link to a directory and clears one of the two; T is an unattached inode or an unconnected directory until (6); between (3) and (4) a moved directory's `..` still names P1. Adding before removing means a crash never loses S. The top of `namespace.cpp` lists the order of every operation.
    - **`fscli`:** `mkdir`, `rmdir`, `mv <from> <to>` (into an existing directory under its own name, otherwise to the path, replacing as `rename` does), `ln [-s] <image>[:N] <target> <path>` (a hard link to a file of the volume, or with `-s` a symbolic link holding `<target>` as given; a directory at `<path>` takes it under `<target>`'s last name) and `readlink`; all work on FAT16 except the links.
    - **Tests:** `tests/ext2_tree_tests.cpp` (new) runs over the `mke2fs` images and requires `e2fsck -fn` to exit 0 after each scenario, with `debugfs` `stat`, `ls` and `cat` reading the results back: directories nested ten deep with a file at the bottom, 120 subdirectories and 12 files in one directory over several blocks, the errors, removal bottom-up with exact link and free counts and the directory keeping its size (on 1 KiB, 4 KiB and no-filetype volumes); Orlov-lite placement on a new `groups` image (1 KiB blocks in eight groups of 1024, `make_ext2_image.sh`); every rename case above, including a deep directory moved and back and a rename whose new record takes the slack of the record before the old one; fast and slow links of 1, 58, 59, 60, 61, 200 and 1023 bytes on 1 KiB and 4 KiB volumes (1023 fills a 1 KiB block but for the terminator, the longest Linux and e2fsck accept, since a target that fills a block exactly has no room for one; a patched link of exactly 1024 bytes is read back whole) with the refusals; hard links surviving the original name, links to symbolic links and FIFOs, `NotPermitted` for directories and `TooManyLinks` on patched counts. A second crash test stops the device after each of the 136 writes of a scenario with mkdir, files, both kinds of link, a hard link, renames within one directory, across directories and over a file, a directory moved to a new parent and then over an empty directory under another, removals and rmdir; `OnlyRoutineRepairs` moved to `tests/ext2_image.cpp`, gained directory counts, unconnected directories and their `..`, and with renames allowed a second name of a directory and a `..` naming the old parent. No directory's link count is ever too low at a cut. The random model test (now `random ext2 file and directory changes match a model`) adds mkdir, rmdir, rename (files, links and directories, onto new and existing names, with the refusals the model predicts), hard and symbolic links, and checks every name, content, link target, listing, `..` and link count. `fs.core:fscli tree` (FAT16), `ext2-mkdir`, `ext2-mv` and `ext2-ln` check the new commands with `e2fsck -fn` and `debugfs`. `filesystem_helpers` gained `NumberedName`, `BytesOfText`, `WriteAt` and `ReadAt`. The new tests add about 8 s under ASan. Step 4 passed `./build.sh unit-test` (914 cases, ASan and UBSan), the standalone core build (124 tests) and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`.
  - **Step 5 done: ext2 mounted read-write in FeatherOS, with errno from the core.** `fscli cp-in` had come with step 3.
    - **Mount modes:** `Mount` (64) takes `(source, target, request)`, `request` pointing to a `User::MountRequest` (`type`, `flags`; `MountReadOnly`), since syscalls carry three arguments; libc's `mount` accepts `MS_RDONLY` (new in `sys/mount.h`) and nothing else, and the `mount` program takes `-r`, `-o ro` and `-o rw`. A mount is read-write unless asked otherwise. `Filesystem::Driver` gained `PrepareMount(MountMode)`, which the VFS calls outside its spinlock before adding a driver (`VFS::Mount` checks the table again after), the hook `OpenForMount` and `IsMountedReadOnly()` (read-only properties, the mode, or a filesystem that refuses writes), which `/proc/mounts`, `VFS::IsReadOnly`, `IsWritable` and every VFS change now use: on a read-only mount a change fails with `EROFS` before the driver sees it. `Drivers::Ext2()` has writable, tracked properties, but `MountExt2` (at boot, through `RegisterExt2`) mounts the core read-only, so a volume nothing has mounted stays clean; `CoreDriver::OpenForMount` unmounts and mounts the core again when the mode changes, falling back to read-only if the read-write mount fails, and the core's own refusals (an unclean volume, a journal, unknown features) leave the mount `ro`. Unmounting syncs; `Files::Quiesce` now calls `VFS::SyncAll()` too, so shutdown and reboot leave every mounted volume clean. Hard links now exist on a writable filesystem (ext2's `SupportsLinks` comes from the core's capabilities).
    - **Errors:** `Filesystem::SyscallError(FeatherDisk::Error)` (`core_driver.cpp`) maps every core error to Linux's `errno` (`Io` and `Corrupt` both `EIO`, `Unsupported` `EOPNOTSUPP`, the rest as named), `CoreDriver` keeps the last one for the new `Driver::LastError()` (default `SyscallErrorFailed`), and the VFS records it, or its own `EROFS`, `EXDEV`, `EBUSY` and `ENOENT`, for `VFS::LastError()`, which `files.cpp` returns where it used to return `SyscallErrorFailed`. New syscall errors `SyscallErrorNoSpace`, `SyscallErrorTooManyLinks` and `SyscallErrorFileTooLarge` (`ENOSPC`, `EMLINK`, and `EFBIG`, which `errno.h` and `strerror` gained with `EMLINK`); a write that fills the volume returns its bytes and the next one fails with `ENOSPC`. Since paths reach drivers resolved, Linux's codes for `.` and `..` are given in `files.cpp` from the path as written (`LastNameOf`): `rmdir` of `.` is `EINVAL`, of `..` `ENOTEMPTY`, a `rename` with either last component `.` or `..` `EBUSY`, on every filesystem. `rmdir` now prints `strerror` instead of always `not an empty directory`. tmpfs and the virtual filesystems still give no reason (`EIO`).
    - **Tests:** the `STORAGE` kernel test (`CheckExt2(boot_count)`) mounts ext2 read-only (reads, `EROFS`), then read-write: the first boot builds `/os` (a 24000-byte file past the direct blocks, a hard link, a symbolic link, a rename into a new directory, a truncation, a file and a directory made and removed) and checks `EEXIST`, `ENOTEMPTY`, `EPERM`, `EINVAL` and `ENOENT` from the core; the second reads it all back and changes it again (a hard-link name and the symbolic link removed, a directory moved to the root, an append). After each boot `tools/smoke/cases/storage.py` copies the partition out of the image, requires `e2fsck -fn` exit 0 and reads the expected files, link counts, link target and listing with `debugfs`; its message is now `sector, FAT16 and ext2 file writes verified`. The shell part `ext2.py` mounts with `-r` for the read-only checks, then read-write for `mkdir`, `write`, `mv`, `ln`, `ln -s`, `ls -l` (two links), `rmdir .`, a non-empty `rmdir` and `mv ..` with their messages, and removes everything again; the shell case then runs `e2fsck -fn` on the partition. Host tests: `an ext2 partition mounted read-write takes changes through the file syscalls` (writes, links, rename, the `.`/`..` codes, `ENOSPC` on a full volume, the changes kept after a read-only remount), `core errors become Linux's errno codes`, the `.`/`..` codes on FAT16 in `file syscalls report why a change failed`; the read-only ext2 tests mount with `MountMode::ReadOnly`.
    - **Left as it was:** `files.cpp` still removes a file that a rename replaces before renaming, so a crash in between loses the old name (the core's own replacing `Rename` is not used), and a directory still cannot replace an empty one (`EEXIST`).
- **Part 4b: ext2 as root (done).** The root is ext2 from the shared core, on the test disk's first partition; FAT16 moved to the second, where its tests keep running, and its driver stays for M19's removable drives and a future EFI system partition.
  - **The test disk:** `tools/create_test_disk.py` still makes a 48 MiB disk with the scratch sector 2047 before the first partition. `vda1`, from sector 2048 to 32 MiB (31 MiB, MBR type 0x83 or the GPT Linux filesystem GUID, name `FEATHEROS`), is ext2 made with `mke2fs -t ext2 -b 1024 -I 128 -L FEATHEROS -E root_owner=0:0 -d <staging directory>` (`make_root_tree`, `make_ext2_volume`): `BLOCK.TXT` (the file `build.sh` passes, `initramfs/block.txt`, now `FeatherOS root volume OK`), empty `home` and `bin` (what the FAT16 root held besides its test files), and `samples/`, Part 3's ext2 tree moved under one directory so the root's own listing stays out of the tests; mke2fs adds `lost+found`. Modes come from the staging directory (`0644` files, `0755` directories); `debugfs` then sets every owner, since `-d` copies the host's: 0 outside `samples/`, 1000 inside (`hello.txt` `0640` in group 100), and link modes `0777` (`owner_of`, `set_owners`). The fixed UUID, hash seed, `E2FSPROGS_FAKE_TIME` and times keep it byte for byte reproducible. `vda2`, from 32 MiB to the end (16 MiB, 33 sectors less on GPT; type 0x06 or Basic Data, name `FEATHERFAT`), is FAT16 with `Read Me First.txt` and an empty `home`, made with mformat's `-c 4` (2 KiB clusters): left to choose, mformat made FAT12 of the GPT layout's 32735 sectors. Program binaries are not put on the disk; the shell test still copies them to `/bin` with `cp`.
  - **Choosing the root:** `VFS::MountBlockDevice` (`vfs.cpp`) mounts both core drivers (`Drivers::MountDisk`, FAT16 read-write, and `Drivers::MountExt2`, ext2 read-only in the core) and puts the one on the lower-numbered partition at `/` with `PrepareMount(ReadWrite)`: the root is chosen by partition order, not by preferring a type, so the host tests' FAT16 fixtures (with an ext2 partition after FAT16) keep a FAT16 root and need no change. Each found volume is registered as its type and partition name (`RegisterVolumeLocked`; `ext2 vda1` and `fat16 vda2` on the test disk), a missing one is unregistered, and the other volume stays out of the tree; a driver mounted somewhere other than `/` keeps its volume when `MountBlockDevice` runs again (`FindVolume`), and a FAT16 or ext2 root of an earlier disk is replaced. FAT16 is not mounted at boot: tests mount it with `mount -t fat16 vda2 /fat` (the kernel test at `/fat`, the shell part read-only at `/mnt`), which needs no fixed mount point and keeps the mount table as it was. `RegisterExt2` is gone.
  - **Read-only fallback:** a volume the core will not write (not cleanly unmounted, errors recorded, a journal, unknown features) still mounts, read-only, with the core's `featherdisk warning: ext2: mounted read-only: ...` line. `VFS::DescribeRoot` (new) gives the root's type, source and mode, and `MountAndVerifyDisk` (`kernel_runtime.cpp`) logs `VFS: / is vda1 ext2, read-write` or, as a warning, `... read-only: the volume refused writes`; with no FAT16 or ext2 volume at all it panics. Startup then reads `/BLOCK.TXT` from whichever root it got (`VFS: /BLOCK.TXT read passed`, which `NORMAL` checks with the root line).
  - **`sync`:** the ext2 superblock stays not clean while changes are unsynced, and the harness stops QEMU from outside, so a session that wrote to the root would leave it unclean: `e2fsck -fn` fails on the descriptors' counts and the next boot mounts read-only. (Since Part 6 step 5 the writeback task syncs the root within about 5 s of a change, so the rule reads: an ext2 volume stopped before the pass that follows its last change is left not clean and mounts read-only until `e2fsck -fy`; `sync`, unmount and shutdown make it clean at once.) Syscall 71 `Sync` (`Files::SyncAll`, `VFS::SyncAll` under the filesystem lock, `SyscallErrorInputOutput` if a driver fails), libc `sync()` in `unistd.h` (synchronous, as on Linux) and the program `sync` came with this part; the shell session runs `sync` first in its last part (`interrupt.py`), after which nothing writes.
  - **Tests:** the `STORAGE` kernel test mounts `fat16 vda2` at `/fat` (`fat16 vda1` and `ext2 vda2` must be `NotFound`) and runs the FAT16 file checks there (`/fat/PERSIST.TXT`, `SCRATCH.TMP`, cluster counts). `CheckRemount` unmounts the built-in mounts and `/fat`, checks that `/` is busy with mounts in it and with `/BLOCK.TXT` open, unmounts the root, mounts `ext2 vda1` at `/` read-only (`CheckExt2ReadOnly`: `/samples` reads back, changes fail with `EROFS`, `ext2 vda2` is `NotFound`), then read-write, mounts the rest again and checks `PERSIST.TXT`. `CheckExt2` builds and changes `/os` (and `/moved-sub`) on the root as in Part 4 step 5, then syncs; the test unmounts `/fat` at the end. `tools/smoke/cases/storage.py` copies the root's partition (`ROOT_START` to `FAT16_START`) for `e2fsck -fn` and `debugfs`, and reads FAT16 at `FAT16_START` with mtools; its shell session mounts `vda2` at `/fat`, works in `/fat/home` as before (long names, case-insensitive lookups) and runs `sync` before its last `ls`. The shell part `ext2.py` mounts FAT16 read-only at `/mnt` (a long name read, `EROFS` from `write` and `rm`, `ls /mnt` failing once unmounted), then reads and writes the root's `/samples` (`1 directory, 5 files, 80119 bytes`); the shell case runs `e2fsck -fn` on the root after the session. Parts that relied on the FAT16 root changed with it: `mounts.py` expects `vda1 / ext2 rw 0 0`, libctest's `symlink` on `/home` now succeeds (it was `EPERM` on FAT16) and a new file there has mode `0666` (FAT16 showed `0644`), and `text_tools.py` names `work/Moved Copy.txt` in its own case, since the root's names are case-sensitive. Host tests: `the first partition's ext2 volume becomes the root, read-only if it refuses writes` (the core's `1k.img` as `vda1` and the FAT16 fixture as `vda2`: an unclean volume gives `vda1 ext2 ro` and `EROFS`, a clean one `rw`, a write leaves `s_state` 0 until `Files::SyncAll` sets it to 1, `fat16 vda2` mounts at `/mnt`, a FAT16 disk then gives a FAT16 root again) and `Sync` in the syscall dispatch test.
  - **Left as it was:** every disk change still waits for an explicit `sync`, unmount or shutdown before ext2 counts the free space on the disk, so a machine stopped from outside after a change leaves the root read-only until `e2fsck` runs on the host (a periodic sync is in `docs/ideas.md`). The root is not chosen by a kernel command line (`root=`).
- **Part 5: ext3 and ext4 read (done).** ext3 is ext2 plus a jbd2 journal, so it reads through the ext2 code. ext4 adds extents, htree directories, CRC32C metadata checksums, 64-bit block numbers and flexible block groups. A volume whose journal needs recovery mounts read-only. Steps: 1. the ext3/ext4 on-disk layout in the core: feature parsing (a journal or any ext4 feature forces a read-only mount, `needs_recovery` logs why), 64-bit and large group descriptors, flexible block groups, large inodes, CRC16 group descriptor and CRC32C metadata checksums (`common/`) verified on read, with host tests over `mke2fs -t ext3` and `mke2fs -t ext4 -O ^extent` images; 2. extents (trees of any depth, unwritten extents read as zeros) and htree directories read by linear scan with checksum tails, so default `mke2fs -t ext4` images read in the core and `fscli`, with host tests incl. large files and directories; 3. the test disk gains ext3 and ext4 partitions that FeatherOS registers (`ext3`/`ext4` types over the shared driver) and mounts read-only, checked by `STORAGE` and a shell part on both architectures, then the full matrix.
  - **Step 1 done: the ext3/ext4 on-disk layout in the core.** Everything is in `src/fs/core/`; the kernel and the test disk are unchanged (step 3), and the type is still `ext2`.
    - **Features:** `ext/superblock.h` names every feature bit. A volume mounts with the incompatible features in `IncompatibleReadable` (filetype, needs_recovery, 64bit, flex_bg, metadata_csum_seed); any other incompatible bit (extent until step 2, meta_bg, inline_data, encrypt, casefold, mmp, journal_dev, compression, ea_inode, large_dir, dirdata) and the read-only compatible bigalloc refuse it with `Unsupported` and a log line of the bits. Compatible and the other read-only compatible features need nothing to be read: ext3's journal inode is never opened, `GroupHasSuperblock` knows sparse_super2's `s_backup_bgs`, dir_nlink's link count of 1 is shown as it is. `WriteRefusal` (`ext2.cpp`) now writes plain ext2 only: a journal, any incompatible bit but filetype, or huge_file, uninit_bg, dir_nlink, extra_isize or metadata_csum make a writable mount read-only with a log line, so the write path never runs on ext3 or ext4 (Part 6). A journal that needs recovery is not replayed: the mount is read-only whatever was asked, and always logs `the journal needs recovery, which is not replayed; recent changes are missing`.
    - **Layout:** `Superblock` has 64-bit block, free and reserved counts (the upper halves with 64bit), `descriptor_size` (32, or `s_desc_size`, a power of two from 64 to 1024, else `Corrupt`), the UUID, checksum type and seed and `s_backup_bgs`; a volume over 2^32 groups is refused. `GroupDescriptor` has 64-bit bitmap and inode table locations, 32-bit counts, `bg_flags`, `bg_itable_unused`, both bitmap checksums and `bg_checksum`, decoded from 32 or 64+ bytes; `Group`, `StoreGroup` and the table's size step by the descriptor size. flex_bg needed nothing more, since inode tables were already located anywhere in the volume. `Inode` gained `i_generation` and its checksum seed. `i_extra_isize` must keep the extra fields inside the inode and 4-byte aligned, else `Corrupt` (on ext2 too). i_size_high stays a regular file's only, as on Linux without large_dir. Inodes flagged encrypted are `Unsupported`, like extents and inline data. With group checksums, an inode of an `INODE_UNINIT` group or past `inodes_per_group - bg_itable_unused` reads as free (`NotFound`) without its table being read, and on a metadata_csum volume an all-zero inode is free too (as e2fsck takes it).
    - **Checksums:** `common/crc.cpp` has table-driven `Crc32c` and `Crc16` in Linux's form (running value in, nothing inverted). `ext/checksum.cpp` has the volume seed (`s_checksum_seed` with metadata_csum_seed, else the UUID's CRC32C), the descriptor checksum (CRC32C's low half, or CRC16 over UUID, group and descriptor for gdt_csum), bitmaps, inodes (the whole inode with `i_checksum_lo/hi` as zeros; only the low half when `i_extra_isize` leaves out `i_checksum_hi`), directory leaf tails (the 12-byte record with file type 0xDE) and hash-index blocks (`dx_tail` after the entry limit). They are verified on read: the superblock in `DecodeSuperblock`/`CheckSuperblock` (`Corrupt`, an unknown checksum type `Unsupported`), every descriptor at mount (`Corrupt`, logged with the group), each inode in `LoadInode` (its block read whole; logged with the inode) and each directory block in `WalkDirectory`, where an index block is told by Linux's rule (block 0 of an indexed directory, or one starting with an unused record over the whole block); a leaf without its tail is `Corrupt`. Decision: bitmap checksums are not checked on read, because reading never loads a bitmap and checking every group's at mount would cost two reads per group; `BitmapChecksum` is tested against mke2fs's values, and Part 6's writer checks each bitmap as it loads it (Linux then marks that group's bitmap corrupt, logs it and allocates elsewhere).
    - **Hashed directories:** they already read linearly (the index hides behind `..` and fake unused records), so step 2 only adds tests over larger ones; their index blocks' checksums are verified now.
    - **Interface and `fscli`:** `FeatherDisk::Ext::DescribeFeatures` (variant `ext2`/`ext3`/`ext4` and the three feature words) and `FeatureName` (e2fsprogs' names) in `include/fs/ext2.h`, implemented in `ext/features.cpp`. `fscli info` prints `format: ext4, features: has_journal ext_attr ...` in dumpe2fs's order under an ext volume.
    - **Tests:** `make_ext2_image.sh` makes three more images: `ext4-blockmap` (`-t ext4 -b 1024 -g 2048 -O ^extent,^64bit`: metadata_csum with its seed, flex_bg, huge_file, dir_nlink, extra_isize, orphan_file, four groups, then `e2fsck -fyD` gives `many/` a hash index), `ext4-64bit` (2 KiB blocks, metadata_csum seeded from the UUID; mke2fs makes 64bit only with extents, so the script runs `tune2fs -O extent`, `resize2fs -b` and `debugfs -R "feature -extent"`, and the files keep their block maps) and `ext4-gdtcsum` (4 KiB blocks, uninit_bg instead of metadata_csum, no journal). `ext2_image_tests.cpp` reads all three with the full `CheckVolume` (big and sparse files through indirect blocks, links, the 300-entry directory, 1006-byte paths), checks that writable mounts of ext3 and the three end read-only with every byte unchanged and no flush, that needs_recovery mounts read-only on ext3 and ext4, and damages `ext4-blockmap` at random as well. `ext4_layout_tests.cpp` covers the refusals (with matching superblock checksums), feature names and variants, the core's checksums against mke2fs's in every group (descriptors, bitmaps of groups that were written, the root inode), damaged superblocks, descriptors (incl. a 64-byte descriptor's upper half), inodes (a byte anywhere in the 256 bytes) and directory leaf and index blocks giving `Corrupt` while the rest still reads, unwritten groups hiding planted inodes until their flag and count are cleared, and a file debugfs flags for extents giving `Unsupported`. `common_tests.cpp` checks CRC-32C (`123456789`, RFC 3720's vectors) and CRC-16/MODBUS and ARC; `ext2_format_tests.cpp` 64-byte descriptors and sparse_super2. `fs.core:fscli ext4-read` runs `info`, `ls` and `cat` on the ext4 images and checks that `cp-in` leaves `ext4-blockmap` unchanged. Step 1 passed `./build.sh unit-test` (933 tests), `quality.sh check` and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`.
  - **Step 2 done: extents in the core.** Everything is again in `src/fs/core/`; the kernel and the test disk are unchanged (step 3).
    - **Extent trees:** `IncompatibleReadable` gained `extent`, so mke2fs's default ext4 mounts (read-only, by `WriteRefusal`). `ext/extent.{h,cpp}` parses nodes: `ParseExtentHeader` (magic 0xF30A, `eh_max` at least 1 and fitting the node, `eh_entries` at most `eh_max`), `ExtentAt` (48-bit start, a length over 32768 marks an unwritten extent of length - 32768) and `ExtentIndexAt` (48-bit child). `Ext2Filesystem::MapExtent` walks from `i_block` down: a root deeper than 5, a child whose depth is not its parent's minus one (which also stops loops), an `eh_max` beyond what a block holds, index entries out of order, an index child or extent outside the volume (`IsDataBlock`, start + length within `blocks_count`), an extent of length 0 or overlapping the previous one, or (with metadata_csum) a tail that does not match the CRC32C of the block up to `12 + 12 * eh_max` (`ExtentBlockChecksumMatches`, seeded by the inode) give `Corrupt`; every node on the way is checked whole, not just up to the entry used. The flag on a volume without the feature is `Corrupt` too, as Linux treats it. A walk returns a `BlockRun` (logical, physical or 0, count): the rest of the extent, or a hole up to the next extent or the next index entry above, so holes and unwritten extents read as zeros.
    - **Readers:** `MapRun` dispatches between the block map (a run of one block, through the unchanged `MapBlock`, which the write path still uses and which still refuses extents) and `MapExtent`, and refuses inline data and encryption with `Unsupported`. `ReadContents` keeps the last run and maps again only when a read leaves it, and still reads consecutive whole blocks in one device call, across runs that continue each other; `WalkDirectory` does the same per directory block (`RecordPlace::physical` is now 64-bit), and slow links map their block through it. A link shorter than `i_block` is fast whatever its flags say. Capabilities report 2^32 blocks as the largest extent file. Hashed directories needed nothing new: they are read linearly, with leaf tails and index-block checksums verified since step 1.
    - **Tests:** the `ext4` image (mke2fs's default with 1 KiB blocks: extent, 64bit, flex_bg, metadata_csum, journal, orphan_file) now passes the full `CheckVolume`. A new `ext4-extents` image (32 MiB, 1 KiB blocks, 6144 inodes) adds `holes.bin` (3 MiB with every eighth block punched by debugfs: an extent tree of depth 2), `fragmented.bin` (400 blocks that debugfs wrote into the gaps of a removed filler directory: depth 1 with one-block extents), `unwritten.bin` (one written block, then 39 blocks `debugfs fallocate` made unwritten, which reuse blocks `holes.bin` left) and `large/` (3000 entries; `e2fsck -fyD` gives it a hash index with one indirect level, over an extent tree of depth 1). `ext4_extent_tests.cpp` checks the parser on built nodes, reads `holes.bin` whole and in 200 random pieces, reads the unwritten extents as zeros after filling their blocks with 0xA5, lists and looks up `large/`, reads a fast link debugfs flags for extents and refuses inline data, and damages the trees (index node magic, entry count, depth, a child pointing at its own node or past the volume, tail checksums, a leaf extent past the volume, at block 0, empty or overlapping, with the tail rewritten so only the damage is wrong, and the root's magic and depth through debugfs) for `Corrupt`, while parts other leaves map still read. `RawVolume` gained `ExtentPath` and `FileBlock`; random damage now also runs over `ext4-extents` with its inodes and tree nodes. `fs.core:fscli ext4-extents` runs `ls`, `cat` and `cp` over the new image (holes against debugfs's `cat`); `ext4-read` reads `ext4.img` too, and `ext2-errors` takes inline_data (set by debugfs) as its refused feature. Step 2 passed `./build.sh unit-test`, `quality.sh check` and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`.
  - **Step 3 done: ext3 and ext4 partitions in FeatherOS.**
    - **Types:** `include/fs/ext2.h` has `Ext3Type()` and `Ext4Type()` beside `Ext2Type()`: one class (`ExtFilesystemType` in `ext/ext2.cpp`) under three names. Each `Probe` takes only volumes of its own variant (`Variant`: ext4 with any ext4 feature, else ext3 with a journal, else ext2, as blkid names them), so probing the three in turn names a volume; each `Mount` takes all three variants, as Linux's ext4 driver does, which kept the core's tests mounting every image through `Ext2Type()`. The core's log lines still say `ext2:`. `fscli` probes FAT16, ext2, ext3, ext4 (`Fscli::FilesystemTypes`), so `info` prints `filesystem: ext3` or `ext4`; `CheckImage` and the random damage test probe with the image's own type.
    - **The test disk:** `tools/create_test_disk.py` makes 64 MiB: `vda1` ext2 root (31 MiB) and `vda2` FAT16 (32 to 48 MiB, now a fixed 16 MiB in both layouts) as before, `vda3` ext3 (48 to 52 MiB, `mke2fs -t ext3 -b 1024 -I 256`, label `FEATHEREXT3`: `hello.txt`, `big.txt` of 3000 `ext3 line NNNNN` lines through an indirect block, `docs/readme.txt`, `link` -> `docs/readme.txt`) and `vda4` ext4 (52 to 60 MiB, mke2fs's default features with `-b 1024 -I 256`, so extent, 64bit, flex_bg, metadata_csum and its seed, a journal and orphan_file; label `FEATHEREXT4`: `hello.txt`, `extents.bin`, `docs/nested/deep.txt`, `link` -> `docs/nested/deep.txt`). `extents.bin` is 2048 blocks of `ext4 block BBBB line LL extents` lines in which every fourth block is zeros; mke2fs leaves zero blocks out, so its 512 extents need a tree of depth 2 (debugfs `ex`). The last 4 MiB are free, which keeps GPT's backup table clear, and an MBR holds exactly these four (0x83, 0x06, 0x83, 0x83; GPT: Linux filesystem GUIDs). Block size and inode size are given explicitly, since hosts' `mke2fs.conf` differ for small volumes; owners are set to 0 and link modes to 0777 with `debugfs` (`set_owners`, now taking the link names and an owner function; `make_journalled_volume`, `write_files`, `finish_tree`, `run_mke2fs`). Fixed UUIDs, hash seeds and `E2FSPROGS_FAKE_TIME` keep both reproducible.
    - **Kernel:** `Drivers::Ext3()`/`Ext4()` are two more `CoreDriver` instances (writable properties, so the core decides and Part 6 needs no change here), found by `MountExt3`/`MountExt4` read-only in the core. `VFS::MountBlockDevice` registers them as `ext3 vda3` and `ext4 vda4` but never puts them at `/`: the root is still the lower-numbered FAT16 or ext2 volume. A mount asking for writing reopens the core mount writable, which the core turns read-only with `ext2: mounted read-only: a journal, which writes would have to go through`, so `/proc/mounts` says `ro` and changes fail with `EROFS`. The registry needs the type of the volume's variant: `mount -t ext4 vda1` is `ENOENT` (Linux would mount ext2 as ext4); with one instance per type, aliases would have had to share the instance, which was left out.
    - **Tests:** `STORAGE` (`CheckExt3AndExt4` in `kernel_test.cpp`) mounts `ext3 vda3` at `/ext3` and `ext4 vda4` at `/ext4` read-write requested (`ext2 vda3`, `ext4 vda3`, `ext3 vda4`, `ext4 vda1` `NotFound`), requires both `ro` in `VFS::DescribeMount`, `EROFS` from remove and create and a failing `mkdir`, reads the files, links (`VFS::ReadLink`), listings, `big.txt` line by line and `extents.bin` block by block (holes as zeros), unmounts, mounts ext4 again read-only and unmounts; its line is now `KERN/STORAGE: sector, FAT16 and ext2 file writes verified, ext3 and ext4 read; boot count N`. `storage.py` hashes both partitions in `Start` and requires them unchanged and `e2fsck -fn` clean after each boot (`HashReadOnlyPartitions`, `VerifyReadOnlyPartitions`), and the shell case does the same after its session. The shell part `ext4.py` (after `ext2.py`) mounts `ext4 vda4` without `-r` (`mount -t ext2 vda4` failing first), checks `vda4 /mnt ext4 ro 0 0`, `ls -l` with the link target, `cat` of a file and through the link, `readlink`, `wc -c` of `extents.bin`, `write` failing with `Read-only file system`, then the same briefly for `ext3 vda3` with `rm` refused. Host tests: `ext3 and ext4 partitions register by their variant and mount only read-only` (the core's `1k`, `ext3` and `ext4` images as `vda2` to `vda4` after FAT16; the partitions' sectors unchanged), and the type names and cross-variant mounts in `ext4_layout_tests.cpp`. Part 5 passed `./build.sh unit-test`, `quality.sh check`, `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"` and the full matrix (`tools/check.sh --arch both --matrix`).
- **Part 6: ext3 and ext4 write (done).** Journal replay and jbd2 transactions (shared by ext3 and ext4), so a crash leaves a volume the host can recover; extent allocation and splitting, and checksum updates. Steps: 1. jbd2 journal replay in the core: parse the journal superblock (v1/v2, 64-bit, checksum v2/v3) and its descriptor, commit and revoke blocks, replay committed transactions on a read-write mount and clear `needs_recovery` (a read-only mount stays read-only and reads the volume as it is on disk), host tests over journals written with debugfs (`jo`/`jw`/`jc`) incl. revokes, a torn last transaction and bad checksums, `e2fsck -fn` clean after replay; 2. writes on unjournalled ext4 layouts: the write path keeps CRC16/CRC32C checksums (superblock, descriptors, inodes, bitmaps checked as they load, directory tails), 64-bit descriptors, flex_bg, uninit groups, large inodes and `dir_nlink`, and adding to or removing from an htree directory keeps the index valid (or clears it as Linux's ext2 does), still block-mapped files only, host tests with `e2fsck -fn` on `mke2fs -t ext4 -O ^extent,^64bit,^has_journal` and 64-bit images, crash and random model tests extended; 3. extent writes: new files get extents on volumes with the feature, appends extend or add extents, splits grow the tree, truncate and delete free extent blocks, unwritten extents written become written, with `e2fsck -fn` and the crash and model tests over default ext4 images without a journal; 4. jbd2 transactions: every metadata change goes through the journal in ordered mode (data before the commit), a commit per sync or operation batch, checkpointing and journal wrap, so ext3 and ext4 with journals mount read-write; crash tests replay at every write and require `e2fsck -fn` clean (design in `docs/design/M22-P6-s4.md`; sub-steps: 4a the log format writer and metadata routing, no behaviour change; 4b transactions, ext3 and ext4 with an internal journal writable in the core and `fscli`; 4c orphans: the orphan file or `s_last_orphan` list makes split writes, truncations and removals crash-atomic, and a writable mount processes every entry; 4d crash and model tests at every write against both our replay and e2fsprogs, `fscli` tests, docs); 5. FeatherOS mounts ext3 and ext4 read-write (the core decides), `STORAGE` writes both and the harness runs `e2fsck -fn`, a shell part, then the full matrix.
  - **Step 1 done: jbd2 journal replay in the core.** Everything is in `src/fs/core/`; the kernel and the test disk are unchanged (their journals are clean).
    - **Format:** `ext/journal.{h,cpp}` decode the big-endian jbd2 structures: the journal superblock (`DecodeJournalSuperblock`, versions 1 and 2; `CheckJournalSuperblock` refuses unknown incompatible features and asynchronous commits without checksums v2/v3 with `Unsupported`, and gives `Corrupt` for a wrong magic or version, both checksum versions, a checksum type other than CRC32C, a superblock checksum that does not match, another block size, `s_maxlen` past the journal inode's size, or `s_first`/`s_start` outside the ring), descriptor tags (`JournalTags`, 8 to 16 bytes by 64BIT, CSUM_V2 and CSUM_V3, with the UUID that follows a tag without `SAME_UUID` and `LAST_TAG`), revoke blocks (`RevokeRecordsFit`, `ReadRevokeRecords`, 4- or 8-byte records) and the checksums: descriptor and revoke tails, commit blocks (`h_chksum[0]` over the whole block) and block tags (CRC32C of the sequence, then the escaped copy, 16 or 32 bits), all seeded by the CRC32C of the journal's UUID. `RevokeTable` is an open-addressing table from the host's memory that keeps, per block, the latest transaction that revoked it; sequences compare with wrap-around (`SequenceBefore`, jbd2's `tid_gt`). The compatible v1 checksums (crc32, `journal_checksum`) are not verified; a reader may ignore them, and debugfs's own writer computes them over revoke-only transactions differently from its recovery.
    - **Recovery:** `Ext2Filesystem::RecoverJournal` (`ext/recovery.cpp`) runs on a read-only instance that `Mount` creates for a mount asked to write when `needs_recovery` is set. It reads the journal inode (`s_journal_inum`) through the block map or extent tree, then makes jbd2's three passes from `s_start`: the scan finds the first transaction without a valid commit block, the revoke pass collects the revoke records before it, and the replay pass writes each tagged block (un-escaped) to its place in log order unless a revoke in its own or a later transaction covers it. Decision, as Linux: only a torn tail ends the log, that is a transaction whose commit block never comes (or, with async_commit, fails its checksum). Every check is in the scan, before any write: a committed transaction whose descriptor or revoke tail, block or (without async_commit) commit checksum fails, that names a block past the volume or whose revoke count runs past its block, as well as a hole in the journal, a log that runs round the whole ring (`JournalLog::Advance`) and a journal superblock that does not hold together, give `Corrupt` with nothing written and `needs_recovery` still set, with a log line that says to run e2fsck (`CheckCommit`, `TransactionCheck`); emptying the journal past such a transaction would lose the ones after it. The errors state is not set. Afterwards it flushes, writes the journal superblock as e2fsck leaves it (`s_start` 0, `s_sequence` one past the first transaction not replayed, its checksum), flushes, clears `needs_recovery` in the superblock the replay may have rewritten (with its CRC32C on metadata_csum volumes; `ClearRecoveryFlag`) and flushes again; a crash anywhere replays the same transactions again or finds an empty journal. `Mount` (`ext2.cpp`, now with `ReadMountable` and `Recover`) then reads the superblock again and mounts as for a clean volume, which with a journal is still read-only until step 4. A read-only mount never writes and reads the volume as it is on disk; a journal on another device, without the journal feature, with unknown features, or a device that refuses writes mount read-only the same way, with the old log line. `Superblock` gained `journal_inode`.
    - **Tests:** `tests/ext_journal_tests.cpp`. Tag sizes, tag parsing (64-bit blocks, UUIDs, the last tag, the room before the tail) and the revoke table (10000 records, latest sequence kept, wrap-around) on built blocks. Over images: debugfs writes (`jo`, `jw -b`, `jw -r`, `jw -c`, `jc` through `ScriptWithDebugfs`, a new helper in `tests/ext2_image.cpp`) five transactions without replaying them: hello.txt's data block and its inode table block from a copy debugfs gave mode 0600, three spare blocks of which the first starts with the journal's magic (escaped), a revoke of the other two, the second written again, and the third once more without a commit. On ext3 (8-byte tags), ext4-blockmap with v2 (10), ext4 (12), ext4 with v3 (16, 64-bit), ext4-64bit with v2 (14) and ext4-blockmap with v3 (16, 32-bit), a writable mount must leave every byte as `debugfs jr` does on a copy (the superblock's times, `s_kbytes_written` and checksum aside), `e2fsck -fn` clean, the journal empty at sequence first + 5, and the mount read-only; a second writable mount changes nothing. A read-only mount and a read-only device change nothing and read the old contents. Damaged checksums in committed transactions followed by valid ones (transaction 2's descriptor tail, its first block, its commit block; transaction 3's revoke tail) fail the writable mount with `Corrupt`, the image byte-identical and unflushed; a damaged descriptor in the uncommitted transaction 5 changes nothing, and with async_commit set a bad commit checksum in transaction 2 ends the log there (transaction 1 replayed, `e2fsck -fn` clean). A tag past the volume, a revoke count past its block, `s_start` or `s_maxlen` outside the journal, another block size or version, a bad journal superblock checksum and a ring full of uncommitted descriptors fail the mount with `Corrupt` and change nothing; unknown journal features and an external journal mount read-only unchanged. An empty journal with `needs_recovery` set only gets the sequence bumped and the flag cleared, as `jr` does. A log rotated to wrap past the ring's end replays like `jr`. Replays stopped at every write (`FailWritesAfter`) fail with `Io`, and the next writable mount reaches the same bytes as an uninterrupted one. The test that needs_recovery mounts read-only (`ext2_image_tests.cpp`) went, since a writable mount now replays. Step 1 passed `./build.sh unit-test` (955 tests), `quality.sh check` and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"` (after the review fix, `--arch x86_64 --modes "STORAGE"`).
  - **Step 2 done: writes on unjournalled ext4 layouts in the shared core.** Everything is in `src/fs/core/`; the kernel and the test disk are unchanged: the test disk's ext3 and ext4 partitions have journals (and ext4 extents), so they still mount read-only in QEMU.
    - **What is written:** `WriteRefusal` (`ext/ext2.cpp`) and the masks in `ext/superblock.h` let a writable mount through with the compatible features dir_prealloc, ext_attr, resize_inode, dir_index and sparse_super2, the incompatible filetype, 64bit, flex_bg and metadata_csum_seed, and the read-only compatible sparse_super, large_file, huge_file, gdt_csum (uninit_bg), dir_nlink, extra_isize and metadata_csum. A journal, the extent feature even when no file uses it (`extents, which only the read path supports`, until step 3), any other incompatible bit and unknown compatible or read-only compatible bits (orphan_file, quota, btree_dir) still mount read-only. Block maps hold 32-bit block numbers, so on a 64bit volume `AllocateBlock` never hands out blocks from 2^32 on, and `CheckReleasable` refuses an extended attribute block with a nonzero `l_i_file_acl_high` (`Inode::attribute_block_high`).
    - **Checksums:** the superblock's CRC32C on every write (`StoreSuperblockChecksum`, now shared with journal recovery); `bg_checksum` (CRC16 or CRC32C over the whole 32- or 64-byte descriptor) in every `StoreGroup`, which stores counts, `bg_flags`, `bg_itable_unused` and both bitmap checksums with their upper halves on 64-byte descriptors (`EncodeGroupDescriptor`, was `EncodeGroupCounts`); each inode's over the whole inode in `StoreInode` (`StoreInodeChecksum`; the low half only when `i_extra_isize` leaves out `i_checksum_hi`); leaf tails on every new or changed directory block, whose records now end 12 bytes early, and the index checksum of a changed index root (`WriteDirectoryBlock`, `StoreDirectoryTail`, `StoreIndexBlockChecksum`; `WalkDirectory` stops before the tail); extended attribute blocks, verified (magic, `h_blocks` 1, `AttributeBlockChecksum` over the volume seed and the block number) before release and rewritten when their reference count drops. New inodes keep `i_extra_isize` 32 (or the superblock's wanted size), nanosecond times and `i_crtime`.
    - **Bitmaps and groups (`ext/allocation.cpp`):** `LoadBlockBitmap` and `LoadInodeBitmap` check a bitmap's CRC32C (over the group's bits; the lower half with 32-byte descriptors) as it loads, which closes step 1's open point. A mismatch logs `ext2: mounted read-only: block bitmap checksum mismatch in group ...`, stops writes on the whole mount (Linux marks only the group) and fails the change with `Corrupt`, which records the errors state, so the next mount is read-only until e2fsck has run. A `BLOCK_UNINIT` group's bitmap is built as Linux's `ext4_init_block_bitmap` builds it (`MarkGroupMetadata`: the superblock and descriptor copies, and any group's bitmaps and inode table inside the group, with the padding bits set) and must agree with the descriptor's free count; an `INODE_UNINIT` group's is empty but for its padding. `CommitBitmap` writes a changed bitmap and keeps its checksum in the descriptor; when the change clears an uninit flag or lowers `bg_itable_unused` (to the new inode, as `__ext4_new_inode` does), it writes the descriptor table at once, before anything uses what the descriptor now covers. Taking an inode from a `BLOCK_UNINIT` group writes its block bitmap too, as Linux does. `IsGroupMetadata` knows flex_bg's packed bitmaps and inode tables (the groups of the flexible group, from the new `Superblock::log_groups_per_flex`). `CheckGroups` also refuses writes for `bg_itable_unused` above the free inodes, `INODE_UNINIT` on a group with inodes in use and `BLOCK_UNINIT` on the last group, as e2fsck does.
    - **Hashed directories:** decision: keep the index where that needs nothing, drop it where it would need hashing. Removing a name, and a rename that points an existing entry at another inode, leave the index right (no hash changes), so it stays; a rename that moves an indexed directory rewrites `..` in its index root with the index checksum. Adding a name drops the index first (`ClearIndex` in `ext/write.cpp`), as Linux's ext2 did; lookups here were always linear. Without metadata_csum only `EXT4_INDEX_FL` goes. With it, the blocks that held the index need a leaf's checksum record: the root's `..` and an interior node's empty record each give up their last 12 bytes. Order, found by testing every cut with `e2fsck -p`: the root first (it then passes as a leaf while e2fsck still follows the index through it), then the flag, then the interior nodes, which until then are blocks without a checksum record that `e2fsck -n` stops at ("directory corrupted, Salvage?") and preen mode salvages. Clearing the flag first, or converting nodes before the flag, left states preen refused. `SearchDirectory` takes no room in index blocks (`RecordPlace` gained `logical` and `index`).
    - **dir_nlink and huge_file:** with dir_nlink, a directory's count past 65000 (`EXT4_LINK_MAX`) becomes 1 and stays 1 when subdirectories go, as `ext4_inc_count`/`ext4_dec_count` keep it (`DirectoryLinks`, `HasSubdirectoryLink`, `LinkLimit` in `ext/namespace.cpp`); `mkdir` and moving a directory never give `TooManyLinks` there, and files take 65000 links (32000 elsewhere). With huge_file, `EncodeInode` stores `i_blocks` in 48 bits and, past them, in blocks under `EXT4_HUGE_FILE_FL`, clearing the flag otherwise, as `ext4_inode_blocks_set`; such volumes no longer cap block-mapped files at 2 TiB of blocks.
    - **Write order:** as in Part 4, a crash leaves only leaks, with checksums written in the blocks they cover; the descriptors' bitmap checksums lag behind their bitmaps until `Sync`, which e2fsck reports as a bitmap that does not match its checksum and repairs in preen mode (added to `OnlyRoutineRepairs`).
    - **Tests:** `make_ext2_image.sh` makes `ext4-csum` (ext4-blockmap without the journal, and so without orphan_file: metadata_csum with its seed, flex_bg, huge_file, dir_nlink, extra_isize, groups 1 to 3 `INODE_UNINIT` and group 2 `BLOCK_UNINIT`, `many/` hashed by `e2fsck -fyD`), `ext4-csum64` (ext4-64bit without the journal; `e2fsck -D` now runs before the 64bit conversion, since e2fsck puts the extent feature back on a 64bit volume, and its note that such a volume "needs extents" counts as noise) and `ext4-htree`/`ext4-htree-gdtcsum` (16 MiB, `large/` with 3000 empty files under a two-level index, with metadata_csum or uninit_bg). The write and tree scenarios (create, write and read; indirect blocks; removing every name of the hashed `many/` and adding new ones; a root grown past 12 blocks; a full volume and no inodes left, across uninit groups; attributes with nanoseconds; nested and wide directories; renames; symbolic links) run over `ext4-csum`, `ext4-csum64` and `ext4-gdtcsum` with `e2fsck -fn` exit 0; the write crash test runs over `1k` and `ext4-csum` (261 cuts), the tree crash test over `1k` and `ext4-csum64` (whose top-level directories land in uninit groups), and the random model test over all three as well. `tests/ext4_write_tests.cpp` (new): which volumes mount writable; one top-level directory per uninit group with its flags, `bg_itable_unused` and free counts checked; a damaged block and inode bitmap (`Corrupt`, then `ReadOnly`, the errors state, a read-only next mount); the hashed directory through removals, a replacing rename and a move and back (index kept, checked with debugfs `htree`), then 100 new names (index dropped), with lookups, listings and e2fsck after each phase on both htree images; a crash test of a removal and three new names in it at every write, which must leave only routine repairs (the salvage lines allowed) and be clean after `e2fsck -fp` (`PreenWithE2fsck`, new in `tests/ext2_image.cpp`); dir_nlink on counts debugfs patched to 64999; huge_file on a file debugfs flagged; an extended attribute block shared by two inodes dropping to one reference and then freed, and a damaged one refused. `ext2_format_tests.cpp` checks `i_blocks` with huge_file and the new descriptor fields. The test that ext4 volumes mount read-only lost `ext4-gdtcsum`. Step 2 passed `./build.sh unit-test`, `quality.sh check` and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`.
  - **Step 3 done: extent writes in the shared core.** Everything is in `src/fs/core/`; the kernel and the test disk are unchanged: the test disk's ext3 and ext4 partitions have journals, so they still mount read-only in QEMU (`STORAGE` and `SHELL` confirm it).
    - **What is written:** `WriteRefusal` (`ext/ext2.cpp`) no longer refuses the extent feature (`IncompatibleWritable` gained it); a journal still makes a mount read-only until step 4. On a volume with extents, `CreateNode` (`ext/namespace.cpp`) gives new regular files (an empty root: no entries, `eh_max` 4, depth 0), directories and slow symbolic links `EXT4_EXTENTS_FL` with their block in an in-inode extent, as Linux's `ext4_new_inode` does; fast links and special nodes get none. Files that already have block maps keep them: the write path picks the mapping per inode, and `MaximumFileSize(extent_mapped)` gives block-mapped files the reach of their map; extent files end a block before 2^32 blocks, as Linux's `ext4_max_size` (the capabilities now say so, one block less than step 2's).
    - **Trees (`ext/extent_write.cpp`, new):** `FindExtentPath` reads the way from the root to the leaf for a logical block (`ExtentPath`, `ExtentLevel` in `ext/ext2_filesystem.h`) with MapExtent's checks (`LoadExtentNode`, now shared) plus nonempty nodes below the root and leaf extents below the next index entry. Every change builds the node's new entries in a list (laid out as a node, room for two more than a node holds) and hands it to `ReplaceExtentNode`, which commits it with exactly one write: in place when it fits and its first logical block stays; otherwise in a new block linked from the level above (copy-on-write, since e2fsck requires an index entry to equal its child's first block), split in two new blocks (or, when entries were only appended, the old node kept and a new sibling started, so appending files fill their leaves), or, at the root, moved whole into a new block under a root one level deeper (`eh_max` `(block size - 12) / 12` in blocks, 4 in the inode, CRC32C tails seeded by the inode on every block written). A node that loses its last entry leaves the level above; an emptied root becomes a depth-0 leaf again, as Linux leaves it. Trees never get shallower otherwise: e2fsck's "could be narrower/shorter" offers are no faults (exit 0, `OnlyRoutineRepairs` treats them as noise). Tree blocks are allocated from the inode's group (`AllocateTreeBlock`), and ones a failed change allocated before its commit go back (`ForgetFresh`).
    - **Writes:** `WriteExtentFile` goes run by run: blocks an extent maps are written in place; unwritten ones get the data (zeros around it in partial blocks, so old contents never show) and then `MarkWritten` splits the extent into up to three, joining the written part to a written neighbour it continues (so writing front to back through fallocated space keeps two extents); a hole gets a run of new blocks after the block before the write (`AllocateBlocks` in `ext/allocation.cpp`, contiguous free bits of one group with one bitmap write; `AllocateBlock` uses it), written and then mapped by `InsertExtent`, which extends the extent before it when the run continues it (up to 32768 blocks) or inserts one. Directories on extents grow by one block at the end (`AppendExtentBlock`); hashed ones drop their index as before (`ClearIndex` maps through `PhysicalBlock`, as `ZeroFrom` and the goal lookups do now). `Truncate` shrinks with `ShrinkExtents` (from the right, one leaf per commit, partial and unwritten extents cut, data and emptied tree blocks freed after the commit); `Remove` frees the whole tree with `ReleaseExtents`. `FinishFrees` now forgets the bitmap it held, since an allocation may change it before the next free (found by the model test: a stale bitmap undid allocations).
    - **Write order:** as before, a crash leaves only leaks: data and new tree blocks are written before the commit that links them, and blocks it unlinks are freed after it. One rule is new: e2fsck ends the range of a tree's last index entry at `i_size` and clears extents past it (not a routine repair), so a write that extends a file stores the larger size before mapping anything (`WriteFile`, restored to what was written if it stops short), and a directory its larger size before mapping its new block; a crash then leaves a file ending in a hole, or a directory size that preen mode cuts. Truncation frees first and stores the smaller size last, as before.
    - **Tests:** `make_ext2_image.sh` makes `ext4-nojournal` (`ext4-extents` without the journal: mke2fs's default ext4 with 1 KiB blocks, 64bit, flex_bg, metadata_csum, `holes.bin` two levels deep, `fragmented.bin`, `unwritten.bin`, hashed `large/`) and `ext4-mixed` (4 KiB blocks, made without extents and given the feature by `tune2fs`, so its files keep block maps). mke2fs adds no orphan_file without a journal. `tests/ext4_extent_write_tests.cpp` (new) checks, with `e2fsck -fn` after each and debugfs `ex` (parsed into levels, extents and the unwritten flag) and `cat`: new nodes' flags and roots; a 3 MiB sequential file (free blocks counted against debugfs's tree) and two files appended in turn until each has over 400 one-block extents (roots of depth 0, 1, then 2), removed to the last block; filling every hole of `holes.bin` (leaf and index splits) and writing in front of a two-level tree (copy-on-write up the left edge); 2000 blocks debugfs fallocated with old contents of 0xA5, written in the middle, front to back, across and past the size, never showing 0xA5; truncation at seven points of a two-level tree, to an empty root, growth with a hole, and `holes.bin` cut and emptied; 2000-entry directories on both images and `large/` taking 200 names; a crash test at every write of its own scenario (a root moving down, a write in front of the tree, holes splitting a full leaf, truncation, a long unwritten extent written in the middle and from its start, a removal, a directory growing; 254 cuts); and 600 random writes and truncations of one file against a model on both images. The write, tree and model scenarios (create, write and read, volume full, attributes, nested and wide directories, renames, symbolic links, both crash tests, the random model) run over `ext4-nojournal`, and some over `ext4-mixed`. The test that extent volumes mount read-only now requires `ext4-nojournal`, `ext4-mixed` and `ext4-csum` with the extent bit writable. Step 3 passed `./build.sh unit-test`, `quality.sh check` and `tools/check.sh --arch both --modes "STORAGE SHELL"`.
  - **Step 4 done: jbd2 transactions in the shared core** (design `docs/design/M22-P6-s4.md`, sub-steps 4a to 4d). Everything is in `src/fs/core/`; the kernel and the test disk are unchanged: the test disk's ext3 and ext4 partitions have journals, so they still mount read-only in QEMU until step 5.
    - **What is written:** ext3 and ext4 volumes with an internal journal (inode 8 in the volume, version 2 superblock, at least 1024 blocks, features revoke, 64bit, checksum v2 or v3, async commit with v3) mount writable in the core and in `fscli`: `WriteRefusal` (`ext/ext2.cpp`) accepts `has_journal`, `orphan_file` together with it and `orphan_present`. Every metadata block (superblock, descriptors, bitmaps, inode tables, directories and hash index blocks, indirect blocks, extent nodes, extended attribute and slow link blocks, orphan file blocks) goes through the journal; file contents are written in place (ordered mode, as Linux). These stay read-only and untouched: a journal on another device, a version 1 journal, one shorter than 1024 blocks or with fast commits or unknown features, a malformed orphan list or orphan file, and a volume without a journal that has orphans.
    - **Log format (4a):** `ext/journal.{h,cpp}` gained the writers for the decoders of step 1: descriptor blocks (`JournalTags` round trips for 8 to 16-byte tags, tag checksums over the escaped copy), revoke blocks, commit blocks, `EncodeJournalFeatures`, `EscapeJournalBlock` (a block that begins with the magic is logged with those 4 bytes zeroed and the ESCAPE flag). `ReadMetadata`, `WriteMetadata` and `WriteData` route every read and write by kind.
    - **Transactions (4b):** `ext/transaction.{h,cpp}`. A change is a step in a layer of block copies (`BlockLayer`, keyed by home block): step, then running (the open transaction, whole steps only), then committed (in the log, not yet home); metadata reads look through them in that order (`Ext2Filesystem::Journalled` runs a step and rolls it back whole on failure or overflow). A commit (`CommitTransaction`) writes the revoke blocks, per group a descriptor and its copies, flushes, writes the commit block and flushes again, so a commit and a checkpoint cost two flushes each; commits happen at `Sync`, at unmount, when the running transaction reaches a quarter of the journal, when a step overflows or after 5 s (the host calls `Sync` every few seconds; step 5 makes the kernel do so every 5 s). `Checkpoint` writes the whole committed layer home, flushes, sets `s_start` to 0 and flushes again; it runs when the ring or the memory (4 MiB of copies) runs short and at unmount. The ring wraps. A freed metadata block that has a committed copy gets a revoke and leaves the layers, and is not allocated again before the freeing transaction commits. `needs_recovery` is set and flushed before any log block of the mount and cleared after the log is empty at unmount; the first sequence number is `s_sequence + 1`. After an `Io` error the journal aborts: nothing more is written and the next writable mount replays. `LimitJournal` and `Activity()` (`JournalLimits`, `JournalActivity`) let tests lower the limits and read counters. A change that does not fit in one transaction (`min(journal - 1, 4 MiB of blocks)`) even alone fails with `NoSpace` and changes nothing.
    - **Orphans (4c):** `ext/orphan.cpp`. Changes too big for one transaction (a large write, a truncation, the removal of a large file, a rename onto one) are split: the first transaction adds an orphan entry (an orphan file slot with its CRC32C block checksum, or the `s_last_orphan` list when the volume has no orphan file or it is full) with the stored target size, the dropped name and zero links, or the old size kept; each piece keeps the entry; the last removes it and completes the change. A writable mount validates every entry before writing (`CheckOrphans`: a loop, an out-of-range or free inode or a bad block checksum keeps the volume read-only) and processes all of them (`ProcessOrphans`: truncate a linked file to `i_size`, release an unlinked inode) in transactions, as Linux's `ext4_orphan_cleanup` and e2fsck's `release_orphan_inodes` do; `orphan_present` follows the slots.
    - **Write order:** data blocks before the commit that makes them reachable (I2), the revoke blocks, descriptors and copies flushed before the commit block (I3), the commit block flushed before the transaction counts as committed, home locations written only by checkpoints from committed copies, the log emptied on disk before `needs_recovery` is cleared.
