The seal alone gives 3.23 MB; with `--gc-sections`, what is left is 1.78 MB of code (0.7 MB of it the C++ runtime, mostly the demangler and metadata), 0.2 MB of relocations and 80 KB of records. At 600 to 1,250 pages, a program fits the 16,384-page limit with room to spare.
- **Part 3: the standard library and runtime (done).** `src/user/swift_runtime/` builds the full `Swift` module from upstream's whole file list (every `EMBEDDED` and `NORMAL` entry of `stdlib/public/core/CMakeLists.txt` plus the SIMD types, without `ObjectIdentifier+DebugDescription.swift`, which needs macro plugins) into an object every program links, `SwiftOnoneSupport` for Debug builds, and the runtime's C++ with the toolchain's `clang++` (the probe's settings: reflection, type printing, stdin, the environment and Unicode data on; Objective-C, `dladdr`, `dlsym` and backtraces off). `SwiftRT-ELF-WASM.cpp` is linked into each program as an object to register its `swift5_*` sections. The `deps/swift` sparse checkout gained `lib/Demangling`, `stdlib/public/{stubs,LLVMSupport,SwiftOnoneSupport,CommandLineSupport}`. `swift.patch` gained the `CTypes.swift` (`Float80` as `CLongDouble` on x86-64 `os(none)`), `Random.cpp` (`arc4random_buf`) and `LibcShims.h` (`malloc_usable_size`) hunks, plus `__featheros__` cases in `CommandLine.cpp`, so `CommandLine.arguments` works (from the C library's argument block) and `CommandLine.executablePath` is `argv[0]`, under `/system/` for a bare name. `swift_runtime/platform.cpp` instantiates `basic_string<char>` and defines the five other libc++ functions. The C library gained `malloc_usable_size` (`malloc.h`), `remainder`, `remainderf`, `rint`, `rintf`, `nearbyint`, `nearbyintf` (exact, host-tested against the host's), libgcc's 128-bit divisions over `src/libc/wide_division.cpp` (host-tested on 20,000 random pairs), and on x86-64 the 19 `long double` functions in `src/libc/arch/x86_64/long_double.cpp`: x87 instructions for rounding, remainders, `sqrtl`, exponentials and logarithms, an exact `fmal` in 256-bit integers, and series for `sinl`/`cosl`, since QEMU's `fsin` and `fcos` are only double-precise (found by `libctest`, whose checks now number 529 on x86-64 and 517 on AArch64). The loader skips `NONE` relocations. GCC's cross drivers ignore `-fuse-ld=lld`, so a custom command links each full-Swift program with the toolchain's `ld.lld` and `--gc-sections` in both build types; Release builds compile the module and programs with `-lto=llvm-full -experimental-hermetic-seal-at-link`. The first program, `fullhello`, prints its arguments through `CommandLine`, runs the standard library's generic code over its own types (sorting, `Dictionary` and `Set` keys, a generic stack, `max`), and with `fail` stops in `fatalError` with the runtime's message; a `precondition` or an index out of range only traps at `-O`, as on other platforms, so the check uses `fatalError`. Stripped, x86-64 / AArch64: 5.4 / 5.0 MB in Debug, 2.7 / 2.4 MB in Release. The shell smoke test runs it on both targets.
- **Part 4: runtime type information (done).** `reflect` (`src/user/reflect.swift`, in `FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_PROGRAMS`) checks what Embedded Swift cannot compile. All of it ran on Part 3's runtime and library unchanged: casts from `Any` to a protocol composition (`any Shape & Named`), to a protocol through a conditional conformance (`Pair<Square>` is a `Shape`, `Pair<Int>` is not), from `[Any]` to `[Int]` element by element and out of an `Optional` boxed in an `Any`; metatype casts to `any Shape.Type` with a static requirement called on the result; `AnyObject` to `any Named`; `catch let error as ParseError`; a parameterized existential `any Collection<Int>`. `Mirror` of a struct, of a class with its `superclassMirror`, of an enum payload, a labeled tuple and a `CustomReflectable`, and `dump` of a class. Key paths held in arrays as `WritableKeyPath` and `PartialKeyPath`, written through, appended, through an optional chain, used as functions, a `ReferenceWritableKeyPath` on a class, and compared and hashed in a `Set<AnyKeyPath>`. `weak` references (one to an `any Named & AnyObject`) that clear when the object goes, `[weak]` captures and `unowned`. Non-`final` generic methods overridden in a subclass, a generic class whose subclass overrides a method and a `class var`, and `required init` called through an `Animal.Type`. `String(describing:)` and `String(reflecting:)` of enums, structs, tuples, optionals, generic types, metatypes, dictionaries, class instances, closures, `AnyHashable` and errors. One fix was needed, for Release builds only. At the sealed link, LLVM's whole-program devirtualization applied virtual constant propagation to `KeyPath.kind`, a `class var` that the `KeyPath` subclasses override with constants. The pass replaces such a call with a load from bytes it stores next to the vtable in the class's metadata global. A generic class's metadata, however, is copied from that global to the heap while the program runs, so the load read whatever follows the copy, and `appending(path:)` stopped in `unsafeDowncast`. The link now passes `-mllvm -wholeprogramdevirt-skip=*` (`FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_LINK_FLAGS`), which turns the pass off; `-wholeprogramdevirt-cutoff=0` does not. That costs about 400 bytes, since the pass's only other work was single-implementation calls through witness tables. `dump`'s triangles show as `???`, because the terminal prints bytes above 0x7E as `?`. Stripped Release sizes, x86-64 / AArch64: 2.74 / 2.46 MB. The shell smoke test runs `reflect` on both targets, and the matrix's Release shell cases run it sealed.
- **Part 5: the other modules (done).** `_Concurrency`, the `Cxx` overlay, and `_StringProcessing`, `_RegexParser` and `RegexBuilder` from swift-experimental-string-processing, built in full mode as upstream builds them, with no new `swift.patch` hunks, so upstream's `Regex` and concurrency run unchanged (`Codable`'s checks moved to Part 6). The full matrix passed (38 cases) at the end of the part.
  - *Step 1 done: `_Concurrency`.* `src/user/swift_runtime/CMakeLists.txt` builds it as upstream does for full Swift, with no new `swift.patch` hunks: the runtime's C++ (`SWIFT_CONCURRENCY_EMBEDDED` off, with `ExecutorImpl.cpp`; the threading package is already in the runtime) and the module from upstream's list plus `DispatchExecutor.swift` and `ExecutorImpl.swift`. Full Swift reaches its executors through an `ExecutorFactory`, even `async main` (`MainActor.executor.enqueue`), so `PlatformExecutorFeatherOS.swift` names upstream's `DispatchMainExecutor` and `DispatchGlobalTaskExecutor`, as Linux does. These are plain Swift over five `swift_dispatch*` functions, which `src/user/swift_concurrency/global_executor.cpp` now provides over its thread pool when built without `SWIFT_CONCURRENCY_EMBEDDED`, besides the `*Impl` hooks it gives Embedded Swift. Delayed jobs can now go to the main queue as well as the global one. The object build became `featheros_swift_runtime_objects`. Programs import `_Concurrency` implicitly (only the modules below it pass `-disable-implicit-concurrency-module-import`) and link it from the archive `featheros_swift_full_concurrency`, so `fullhello` carries none of it. `tasks.swift` and `executors.swift` build unchanged as `fulltasks` and `fullexecutors` (`FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_PROGRAMS`); only their output prefix depends on `#if $Embedded`, and the shell smoke test expects every Embedded line again with `full` in front. The first run found a Part 3 bug: swiftc imports `SwiftShims` from its resource directory, so the toolchain's unpatched `LibcShims.h` shadowed `swift.patch`'s, `_swift_stdlib_malloc_size` returned 0, and `ManagedBuffer.capacity` was negative in every full-Swift program (`AsyncStream`'s `_Deque` asserted on its first append). Full-Swift builds now pass `-resource-dir` for a directory of symbolic links to the toolchain's `lib/swift`, with `shims` from `deps/swift`, as upstream's build tree has; `fullhello` checks a `ManagedBuffer`'s capacity. Stripped Release sizes, x86-64 / AArch64: `fulltasks` 3.18 / 2.86 MB, `fullexecutors` 2.96 / 2.68 MB (`fullhello` 2.65 / 2.38 MB). Checked by the shell smoke test in Debug and Release on both targets.
  - *Step 2 done: `Cxx`.* `src/user/swift_runtime/CMakeLists.txt` builds upstream's overlay from `deps/swift/stdlib/public/Cxx` in full mode, unpatched (the `import Builtin` hunk is for Embedded Swift only), with upstream's flags plus `LifetimeDependence` and `NonescapableTypes`, into the module `Cxx` and the archive `featheros_swift_full_cxx`. `featheros_add_full_swift_program` gained a `CXX` option for the new list `FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_CXX_PROGRAMS`: the Embedded C++ programs' flags, libc++'s headers and `swift_cxx/module.modulemap`, with the program's C++ sources (`FEATHEROS_SWIFT_CXX_SOURCES_<name>`) built by GCC into an object library and `featheros_user_support` and the overlay in the `ld.lld` link group. Release links first failed with `unable to find library from dependent library specifier: swiftCxx`: C++ interoperability makes the compiler request `swiftCxx`, which in an ordinary object sits in `.swift1_autolink_entries` (ignored by `ld.lld`), but in bitcode becomes an ELF dependent library; these programs now pass `-disable-autolink-library swiftCxx`. `cxxinterop.swift` builds unchanged as `fullcxxinterop` (its output prefix follows `#if $Embedded`), and every check passes as in Embedded Swift, including copy constructors and destructors through value witnesses, the noncopyable type, the shared reference, `std::optional`, `std::vector`, `std::span` and `std::string_view`. `http` stays Embedded, since it imports `FeatherSupport` (Part 6). Stripped sizes, x86-64 / AArch64: 5.48 / 5.07 MB in Debug, 2.65 / 2.38 MB in Release, the same as `fullhello` to within 0.1 MB. Checked by the shell smoke test in Debug and Release on both targets.
  - *Step 3 done: regular expressions.* `build.sh` fetches swift-experimental-string-processing at `swift-6.4.2-RELEASE` into `deps/swift-experimental-string-processing` (sparse: `Sources/_CUnicode`, `_RegexParser`, `_StringProcessing`, `RegexBuilder`; `FEATHEROS_STRING_PROCESSING_SOURCE`, required with the other sources for any Swift program). `src/user/swift_runtime/CMakeLists.txt` builds the three modules unpatched, with the flags of the toolchain's own `.swiftinterface` files (`AllowRuntimeSymbolDeclarations` for `_RegexParser`, which declares `swift_getTupleTypeMetadata` itself; `-D RESILIENT_LIBRARIES` for `_StringProcessing`, as upstream), into the archive `featheros_swift_full_string_processing` with `_CUnicode`'s two C files (script tables reached through `@_silgen_name`); `featheros_swift_runtime_objects` now compiles `.c` sources as C with `c_flags`. Programs import `_StringProcessing` implicitly, so `-disable-implicit-string-processing-module-import` moved from `FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_FLAGS` to the modules built here (`below_string_processing`, part of `below_concurrency`), and programs compile with `-enable-bare-slash-regex`, since Swift 5 mode leaves `/.../` literals off. Everything worked on the first run: `fullregex` (`src/user/fullregex.swift`, `FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_PROGRAMS`) checks literals with named, numbered and optional captures, the `String` algorithms (`firstMatch`, `wholeMatch`, `prefixMatch`, `matches`, `ranges`, `contains`, `starts(with:)`, `trimmingPrefix`, `split`, `replacing` with a closure), back-references, lookahead, reluctant repetition, `ignoresCase`, `anchorsMatchLineEndings`, scalar against grapheme semantics, `\p{Script=Greek}`, run-time patterns as `AnyRegexOutput` and with a checked `Output` type, an invalid pattern, and `RegexBuilder` with `ChoiceOf`, `Optionally`, an anchor and a `TryCapture` read through a `Reference`. Swift has no `~=` for regexes (`case /.../:` does not compile). `Regex` is large: stripped Release sizes, x86-64 / AArch64, `fullregex` 4.82 / 4.34 MB against `fullhello`'s 2.65 / 2.38 MB. `Codable` needs no module of its own in full Swift (the protocols and synthesized conformances are in `Swift`); its checks come with `FeatherSupport`'s `JSONEncoder` and `JSONDecoder` in Part 6. Checked by the shell smoke test in Debug and Release on both targets. One AArch64 Release run failed before `fullregex`, unrelated to it: the first `cp /system/upper /bin/shout` reported `No such file or directory` while later copies to `/bin` worked; the re-run passed (session kept in `build/aarch64/smoke-output-failed-cp.txt`).
- **Part 6: programs and comparison (done).** `FeatherSupport` is built a second time, from the same sources, in full mode (`featheros_add_full_swift_support` in `src/user/CMakeLists.txt`, archive `featheros_swift_full_support` in every full program's link group). `CommandLine` and the several-item `print` became Embedded only (`#if $Embedded`), since full Swift's library has both. The JSON coders were written against the Embedded library's `EncoderBase`/`DecoderBase`; the new `swift_support/codable_containers.swift`, compiled only in full Swift, defines those classes and `CodingSlot` in `FeatherSupport` with the same API, conforming to upstream's `Encoder` and `Decoder` through private container structs that implement every requirement over the slot methods, so `json_encoder.swift` and `json_decoder.swift` are unchanged. Primitives reach the format through a cast to the internal protocol `SlotPrimitive`; a small integer's `typeMismatch` names the type asked for, not `Int64`. Programs compile in other directories' files through `FEATHEROS_SWIFT_SHARED_SOURCES_<name>` in full mode too. The ports `fulljson`, `fulllife`, `fullswiftbench` and `fullhttp` (in `FEATHEROS_SWIFT_FULL_CXX_PROGRAMS`) are the Embedded sources unchanged and worked on the first run; `swiftbench`'s help no longer says Embedded. The new `fullcodable` (`src/user/fullcodable.swift`) checks `Codable` over upstream's protocols: `hello`'s `Milestone` encodes to the same JSON text as in Embedded Swift, upstream's error descriptions with paths and types, `CodingKeyRepresentable` keys, `Double` keys as pairs, `[any Encodable]` with opened existentials, a subclass through `superEncoder`/`superDecoder`, a pretty-printed generic `Pair<Int128>` and a `UInt128` beyond 64 bits. The shell smoke test runs them with arguments whose output differs from the Embedded checks' (it matches text anywhere in the session), and animates a blinker with `fulllife`. `swift.codable.full` (CTest) builds `codable_tests.swift` with the JSON coders and the adapter against the host's full library; since macOS runs it on the system's Swift runtime, which words descriptions its own way, it compares errors by their parts and only checks that the three messages the library writes itself fail. `tools/benchmark.py` runs `fullswiftbench` as a third column and reads sizes from the Release build's programs (`--programs`, passed by `build.sh`; it had read `build/<arch>`'s Debug ones since M28). Its defaults dropped from three rounds of 3 s to two of 1 s, under a minute per architecture, with the same ratios within the usual 10 %. Results (`docs/development.md`, "Swift against C++"): full Swift is 3 to 13 % faster than Embedded Swift on `life` and `sieve`, 9 to 31 % slower on `quicksort` and `json`, and its `sort` is 2.5 times faster (not investigated); its code is about 2 MB against Embedded's 0.2 MB. Stripped Release sizes, x86-64 / AArch64: `fullcodable` 3.05 / 2.76 MB, `fulljson` 3.04 / 2.74, `fulllife` 2.68 / 2.42, `fullswiftbench` 2.72 / 2.44, `fullhttp` 2.70 / 2.42 MB; Debug 5.3 to 5.8 MB.
  - *The seal broke `Codable` in Release.* The first matrix failed both Release shell cases: `fullcodable` and `fulljson -t` stopped with a CPU exception and no output. Every entry of the witness table of `FeatherSupport`'s private `SingleSlotDecoder` was null, and so were the `CodingKey` entries of the synthesized, private `CodingKeys` of `fullcodable`'s types. swiftc marks a private type's vtables and witness tables as visible only within its file, and with the seal's virtual function and witness method elimination on, LLVM's `GlobalDCE` in each module's own compile dropped every entry that file did not call. The conformances escape as existentials, though (`any SingleValueDecodingContainer` to the program, `any CodingKey` to `FeatherSupport`'s encoder), whose calls then went to address 0. Modules and programs now compile with `-Xllvm -enable-vfe=false`, so only the link, where the whole program is one module, removes entries; the programs are the same size as before. Without witness method elimination they were 0.9 MB larger, and with only virtual function elimination 35 KB smaller than that. The full matrix then passed (38 cases in 341 s).

**Complete when:**
- A full-Swift program runs everything on M25's out-of-scope list except Foundation and compiling Swift on FeatherOS, on both targets.
- Upstream's `Regex`, `Codable` and concurrency pass their checks in full mode.
- The matrix covers the full-Swift programs, and `docs/development.md` explains when to choose each mode, with the sizes and benchmark ratios.

### 28. A Smaller Smoke Test And A Parallel Matrix

The QEMU matrix (`tests/test_boot_matrix.py`, 38 cases) took 30 minutes (1825 s on 2026-11-09), almost all of it QEMU time: a rebuild with nothing changed takes under a second, and only the two Release cases rebuild everything (about 1.5 minutes each). `tools/smoke_test.py` had grown to 3,542 lines, with every mode's input, end markers, run-time hooks and checks spread over one `main()` as `if args.expect_...` branches.

**Why the cases cannot run side by side today:** each case is a different kernel, since the `FEATHEROS_*_TEST` CMake options compile the test hooks in (58 `#ifdef` sites in 12 files), and every case runs `cmake --fresh` into the same `build/<arch>`. The cases also share `barebones*.iso` and `iso_root/` in the repository root (`iso_root/` even between the architectures), `build/<arch>/virtio-disk.img` (written by the storage cases), `virt.dtb`, `initramfs/` and `smoke-output.txt`, and each ISO build runs `make -B -C deps/limine`. Host ports (`FreeTcpPort`) and the QMP socket (a temporary directory) are already private to a run.

- **Part 1: split the smoke test by case (done).** `tools/smoke_test.py` keeps the command line only (65 lines, the same arguments, so `build.sh` did not change); the harness is the package `tools/smoke/`, 47 files, the largest 206 lines. Shared pieces: `session.py` (QEMU process, the read/type loop, the `Result` with output, captured bytes and the limit note; it still saves `build/<arch>/smoke-output.txt`), `script.py` (`WholeWrite`, `TypeWhen`, `WaitFor`, `WaitUntil`, Ctrl+C/Z while a command runs, `Lines`), `qemu.py` (`Command`, QMP), `terminal.py` (escape and prompt patterns), `boot.py` (boot messages), `build_tree.py` (`ProgramBuilt`), and `hosts/` with one file per server or client (DNS, NTP, echo, HTTP, lossy relay, forwarded client, httpd client). Each mode is a `Case` subclass in `cases/` (`base.py`: `timeout`, `serial_input`, `reboots`/`shuts_down`, `Start`/`Stop` around the run, `NetdevOptions`, `QemuArguments`, `QmpSocket`, `Launched`, `Script`, `Markers`, `OnOutput`, `Finished`, `Check` returning a failure message, `PassMessage`); `KernelTest` covers the one-line kernel hooks, `BootToShell` the normal boot and timer modes (which, as before, end on the shell banner and the DHCP lease). `cases/__init__.py` maps each `--expect-<mode>` switch to its case and now refuses any two modes, `--expect-power` and `--expect-storage-boot` included. The network case is `cases/network/` (`script.py`, `expected.py`, `checks.py`). The shell mode types 22 parts from `cases/shell/` in `PARTS` order: the four Swift parts (each with `requires=` and a note when the build skipped it), `programs`, `files`, `ping`, `text_tools`, `pipes`, `jobs`, `restart`, `limits`, `workers`, `cppbench`, `calculator`, `editing`, `vi`, `top`, `keys`, `script_command`, `job_control` and `interrupt`, whose last line's output ends the session. The expected `^C` and `^Z` counts are summed from the parts. A comparison script checked that the typed input, every expected text and pattern, and the QEMU command line and stdin of all 17 modes on both targets were unchanged; the matrix passed (38 cases, 1854 s against 1825 s before).
- **Part 2: a faster, parallel matrix (done).** Measured first: typing each character 20 ms apart was 95 s of every ~3-minute shell session and 67 s of every network session, about 12 of the matrix's 31 minutes, and the Release cases rebuilt everything in `build/<arch>` (and the next Debug build again). Four changes:
  - *Whole lines.* `session.py` writes each scripted line at once when a prompt asks for it (QEMU takes bytes from the pipe only as the UART has room, and the terminal queue holds 256 bytes), so `WholeWrite` went away.
  - *Ctrl+C and Ctrl+Z on time.* A key for a running command goes 0.5 s after the shell echoed the command's newline (`COMMAND_START_SECONDS`) instead of 1.5 s after typing; the session keeps polling while the command prints nothing.
  - *A folder per build type.* Release builds go to `build/<arch>-release`. `build` now also makes what all images of a build share (`_image_inputs`: the initramfs, Limine's tool, AArch64's `virt.dtb`); `iso` writes the ISO (`featheros.iso`, no longer `barebones*.iso` in the repository root), `iso_root` and a fresh disk to `OUT_DIR` (the build folder by default); `smoke` runs `iso` and the smoke test without building. `smoke_test.py` takes `--build-dir` and `--session-output`.
  - *Test hooks chosen at boot.* The eleven `FEATHEROS_*_TEST` CMake options and `FEATHEROS_KERNEL_TEST` are gone: `kernel_test.cpp` is always built, and `KernelTest::Mode` comes from `test=<mode>` on the Limine kernel command line (`limine_kernel_file_request`), parsed in `kmain` right after serial starts, before exceptions are set up (x86-64 gives the divide-error test its IST stack only in that mode); an unknown mode panics. The hooks are built only with `FEATHEROS_KERNEL_TESTS` (`KERNEL_TESTS=ON`, off by default, on for `test`, `smoke`, `test-all` and `check.sh`): it compiles `kernel_test.cpp` and `kernel_test_mode.cpp` and defines the macro, which guards every test-only line elsewhere (the exception handlers' fault recovery for the memory test, x86-64's divide-error IST stack, `Console::WriteSerialLine`, the user-preemption and per-mode calls in `kernel_runtime.cpp`, the kernel-file request and parsing in `kmain`). A kernel without it ignores `test=`, `iso` refuses to make such an image, and it is about 23 KB (x86-64) or 25 KB (AArch64) of Debug code smaller, with 16 KiB less stack per CPU on x86-64. `quality.sh` checks only the sources in `compile_commands.json`, so the hooks are linted in builds that have them (as `check.sh` makes). `src/kernel/kernel_test_mode.cpp` parses it (CTest: `Kernel test mode ...`). `iso` appends `cmdline: test=<mode>` to the image's `limine.conf` from the same `*_TEST` toggles (`_kernel_test_mode`, at most one).
  - *The runner.* `tools/matrix.py` replaced `tests/test_boot_matrix.py` (same 38 case names): it builds each architecture and build type once, then runs `build.sh smoke` for three cases at a time (`-j`, `MATRIX_JOBS`), the longest first, each in `build/matrix/<case>/` (`log.txt`, `smoke-output.txt`); `-k` selects cases, `--no-build` reuses the builds. `build.sh test-all` runs it.
  - *Result:* 38 cases in 451 s against 1854 s, of which the first Release builds into the new folders took 248 s; shell sessions take 36 to 71 s instead of about 3 minutes, and the 22 short cases together 49 s.
- **Part 3 (maybe): split the shell case** into two or three QEMU sessions that run at once, where its parts do not depend on files made by earlier ones. Less needed now: the shell cases are under 75 s, and the Release builds are the longest step. Starting the Debug cases while the Release builds run would help more.

**Complete when:**
- `tools/smoke_test.py` is a thin entry point and no file under `tools/smoke/` is much past 400 lines.
- The matrix runs its cases side by side on both targets and passes, with the time measured before and after in this section.

### 29. The Root As A System Disk (Complete)

**Summary:** the ext4 root is now the system disk. The boot reads no test file from it, `/etc` is its own persistent directory (filled from the initramfs's defaults, edits survive reboots), `/sbin/init` and `/bin` hold the system's programs ahead of the initramfs's copies, which stay as the rescue system and the built-in defaults, and a mount can change its mode in place (`mount -o remount,ro|rw`, the root included), with `/proc/mounts` showing `ro` once a filesystem refuses writes.

Since M22 the root is ext4 on the disk, read-write and journalled, but the system still lives in the initramfs: programs, `init.conf` and `/etc` come from it, `/etc` is a RAM copy whose edits are lost at reboot (since M10 Part 10, `VFS::LoadEtc` into tmpfs's second root, sharing `/tmp`'s 128 entries and 4 MiB). This milestone makes the root hold the system, with the initramfs as the built-in defaults and a rescue fallback when the disk is missing or broken. Developed on `feat/disk` after M22, at the user's request.

- **Part 1: a root without test files (done).** The boot reads no file from the root any more: the `/BLOCK.TXT` check left `MountAndVerifyDisk` in `src/kernel/kernel_runtime.cpp` and became `CheckRootFile` in the `STORAGE` kernel test (`kernel_test.cpp`, same size and contents, run before `CheckRoot`), so the `NORMAL` mode no longer expects the `VFS: /BLOCK.TXT read passed` line (`ROOT_FILE_MESSAGE` removed from `tools/smoke/boot.py`). A root without `/home` already started `init` and the shell in `/` (`Process::Spawn` checks `Files::CheckDirectory("/home")`), so no kernel change was needed there; the shell's `cd` without an argument still names `/home` and prints its error on such a root. `tools/smoke/cases/root.py` no longer patches the partition with `debugfs -w`: the ext3 (`vda3`) and ext2 (`vda4`) roots boot as they are on the test disk, with no `BLOCK.TXT`, `/home` or `/bin`, and the case checks that `pwd` prints `/`, writes `/rootwrite.txt`, shuts down, and that `debugfs` finds none of the test files on the root. A new case boots `KERNEL_ROOT=vda1` (`ROOT_TEST=ON`) after the harness made the disk copy's first partition a new ext4 volume with `mke2fs -t ext4 -b 1024 -I 256`, holding only `lost+found`: it reaches the shell in `/`, takes the write, and the volume is clean under `e2fsck -fn` and `debugfs` after shutdown. Matrix cases 45 and 46, `empty_ext4_root`, on both architectures (the matrix has 46 cases).
- **Part 2: persistent `/etc` and `/etc/init.conf` (done).** `/etc` is the root's own directory when the root is writable: at boot, each file of the initramfs's `etc/` that the root's `/etc` lacks is copied there (a first boot fills it, a deleted file comes back as the default), and nothing is overwritten. `init.conf` moves into the initramfs's `etc/`, so `init` reads `/etc/init.conf`; `/system/init.conf` stays in the initramfs as the built-in copy that `init` falls back to when `/etc/init.conf` is missing or does not parse. Without a disk, or with a read-only root, `/etc` stays the tmpfs copy as today. Edits to `hosts`, `resolv.conf` and `init.conf` survive a reboot, checked by `STORAGE`'s two boots. The shell's `cd` without an argument goes to `$HOME` (`cd: HOME not set` when it is unset) instead of the hard-coded `/home`, and `init` sets `HOME` for the console shells as `login` does on Linux and the BSDs: `/home` when it exists, else `/`. Steps: 1 the kernel's `/etc` on a writable root (missing initramfs defaults copied in at boot, the tmpfs copy otherwise; `STORAGE` checks edits to `hosts` and `resolv.conf` across its two boots); 2 `init.conf` in `/etc` with the `/system/init.conf` fallback, `HOME` from `init` and the shell's `cd` to `$HOME`. (Step 1 done: `VFS::UseRootEtc` (`vfs.cpp`, called by `MountAndVerifyDisk`) makes `/etc` the writable root's own directory and copies the initramfs's missing `etc/` files in, logging `VFS: /etc is the root's own directory`; `STORAGE`'s `CheckEtc` checks edits across its two boots and a deleted default coming back, the harness reads them with `debugfs` (`VerifyRootEtc`). Step 2 done: `initramfs/etc/init.conf` is the one file; `build.sh` installs it at `/etc/init.conf` and `/system/init.conf`. `init` (`src/user/init.cpp`, `LoadConfig`) reads `/etc/init.conf` and, when it is missing or lists no service (a file of malformed lines included), prints `init: /etc/init.conf is missing or lists no service; using /system/init.conf` and parses the built-in copy, then the one-line shell configuration if that fails too. `init` sets `HOME` (`SetHome`: `/home` when it is a directory, else `/`) unless the configuration has its own `env HOME=` line, which wins; the default configuration no longer has one. The shell's `cd` alone goes to `$HOME`, `cd: HOME not set` when unset. `STORAGE` also edits `init.conf` (a comment line, read by init at once and kept on boot 2); `ROOT_TEST` checks on the ext3, ext2 and empty ext4 roots that `HOME=/` and `cd` alone returns to `/`, and the empty ext4 root starts with garbage in `/etc/init.conf`, so init must print the fallback line, still reach the shell, and leave the file unchanged.)
- **Part 3: programs from the root's `/bin` (done).** The build installs the programs into the test disk's `/bin` (`tools/create_test_disk.py`'s staging tree), `PATH` becomes `/bin:/system`, and the kernel starts `/sbin/init` when the root has it, else `/system/init` (as Linux, OpenBSD and XNU start `/sbin/init` or `/sbin/launchd`), so the initramfs remains the rescue system. Only `init` goes to `/sbin` in this part; the other programs stay in `/bin`. A program on the root runs ahead of the initramfs's copy, and deleting it falls back to the built-in one. (Done: `build.sh` strips each program once into the initramfs's `system/` and a copy in `build/<arch>/disk-programs/bin` (`init` moved to `sbin`), and `tools/create_test_disk.py --programs DIR` installs both into the root's staging tree (owner 0, mode `0755`; `ROOT_DIRECTORIES` gains `sbin`); `PATH` is `/bin:/system` in `init.conf`, libc's `posix_spawnp` default and the shell's help. The programs of a Debug build total about 75 MB, which did not fit the 31 MiB root, so the test disk grew to 160 MiB: root 127 MiB (`-J size=1` keeps the 1 MiB journal), FAT16 from 128 MiB, ext3 from 144 MiB, ext2 from 148 MiB, with 43 MiB free on the root; the harness imports the offsets from `create_test_disk.py`. The kernel (`kernel_runtime.cpp`) tries `Process::Spawn("/sbin/init")` and, for any failure (missing, not a program, not loadable), starts `/system/init`, logging `Init: starting /sbin/init from the root` or `Init: starting /system/init from the initramfs`; it makes the same choice each time init is restarted, so a `/sbin/init` that crashes later is restarted, not replaced. A program found in `/bin` gets `/bin/<name>` as `argv[0]` (the one-line spawn command carries the path; `libctest`'s check accepts both). `NORMAL` requires the `/sbin/init` line, `ROOT_TEST` the `/system/init` line on the ext3, ext2 and new ext4 roots (the new one holds a garbage `/sbin/init` too), and the shell part `root_programs.py` lists `/sbin` and `/bin`, copies `date` over `/bin/echo` and sees it run ahead of `/system/echo`, deletes it and sees the fallback, then restores it.)
- **Part 4: mount state (done).** `mount -o remount,ro` and `remount,rw` (libc `MS_REMOUNT`, 32) change a mounted filesystem's mode in place, the root's too, and `/proc/mounts` shows `ro` as soon as a filesystem refuses writes. Design `docs/design/M29-P4.md`, in three steps.
    - *Step 1, the core:* `Filesystem::MakeReadOnly` (default `Unsupported`, `src/fs/core/include/fs/filesystem.h`). ext (`Ext2Filesystem::MakeReadOnly`, `ext/ext2.cpp`) runs `Sync` then `CloseJournal`, so ext3/ext4 leave an empty log with `needs_recovery` clear and ext2 is marked clean, while an instance that writes nothing (read-only, fallen back, aborted) writes nothing; FAT16 flushes. Host tests: `tests/ext_remount_tests.cpp` (clean while mounted, rw to ro to rw replaying nothing, a stop at every write of the close, an aborted journal, instances that write nothing; ext2, ext3, ext4, ext4-4k) and the FAT16 cases in `fat_tests.cpp` and `fat_mtools_tests.cpp`.
    - *Step 2, the kernel:* `Driver::Remount`, `RefreshMode`, `RemountReplays`, `ReopenForRemount`, `RefusesWrites` and their `CoreDriver` overrides, `MountResult::ReadOnly`, `VFS::Remount`, `VFS::MountedAt`, `Files::Remount`. `RefreshMode` runs after every change, sync pass and detach, so `/proc/mounts` says `ro` from the call in which a journal aborted (this removes M22-P6-s5's D3). Decisions: a read-only remount is `EBUSY` while a file on the mount is open for writing, a read-write one that would replay a journal while any file is open; a read-write remount the core refuses is `EROFS` and the mount stays `ro`; refusal after a mount is sticky (only `umount` and `mount` bring it back); ext2 without a journal stays `rw` after an I/O error, each change `EIO`; `/system` and `/proc` give 0 for ro and `EROFS` for rw, `/dev` `EINVAL`. A failing data write alone gives `EIO` without aborting the journal; only a failed commit makes the mount read-only. Kernel host tests in `filesystem_tests.cpp` and `virtual_files_tests.cpp`.
    - *Step 3, ABI and tests:* `MountRemount` (`1 << 5`) in `src/user/syscall_abi.h`, `MountSyscall` in `process.cpp` (unknown bits `EINVAL`; a remount copies only the target), libc `MS_REMOUNT` (`source` and `filesystemtype` may be null), and the `mount` program's `-o` comma list (`ro`, `rw`, `remount`; `remount` needs `ro` or `rw`, else it prints the usage). Tests: `syscall_tests.cpp` (the null source passes through; the bit-checking in `MountSyscall` is only exercised by QEMU, as `process.cpp` is not host-tested); `STORAGE`'s `CheckRootRemount` (the root and `/fat` remount in place, the superblock read from the disk shows `needs_recovery` clear, `/etc` follows the root, `/tmp` does not); `STORAGE`'s second-boot shell ends with `mount -o remount,ro /` and `VerifyJournalled` now insists on a clean root that passes `e2fsck -fn` without a replay and holds the file written between remounts; the `SHELL` part `shell/remount.py` (after `mounts`, whose `MOUNT_LINE` count stops at its own end marker). The full matrix runs once for the milestone (no new cases).

**Complete when:** on both targets, a boot from the ext4 root runs `/sbin/init` and the programs from the root's `/bin`, reads `/etc` from the root and keeps its edits across reboots, boots a root with no test files, and still boots from the initramfs alone when the disk is missing; the root can be remounted read-only and back; `e2fsck -fn` finds no errors after each case.

### 30. Storage And System Services

M29 made the ext4 root the system disk; this milestone removes the limits that came with it and gives the administrator's programs and services their own places. Planned on `feat/disk` after M29, at the user's request, and merged into `main` with M22 and M29.

- **Part 1: higher descriptor and open-file limits (done).** A process has 256 descriptors by default and `setrlimit(RLIMIT_NOFILE)` raises the soft limit to 1024 (the hard limit and its ceiling), and 1024 distinct disk, `/tmp` and `/etc` files can be open system-wide; `EMFILE` at a process's limit and the new `ENFILE` at the system's. Design `docs/design/M30-P1.md`.
    - *Step 1:* `Files::Table` (`src/kernel/files.{h,cpp}`) is a heap array of `capacity` slots with `soft_limit` and `hard_limit` (`User::DefaultDescriptorLimit` 256, `MaximumDescriptorLimit` 1024; `MaximumDescriptors` and `MaximumPollDescriptors` are gone): it starts at 32 slots and doubles (`GrowLocked`, capped at the soft limit), `FreeSlotLocked` and `Duplicate` bound new numbers by the soft limit (`dup2` past it `EBADF`, `F_DUPFD` past it `EINVAL`), `CloseAll` frees it, and `Inherit` allocates before taking references and copies the limits (`Spawn` gives `NoResources` when it or `OpenConsole` fails). The disk-file table is a heap array of 64 growing to 1024 (`GrowDiskFilesLocked`, `ReserveUseLocked`, which `Open` also calls before `VFS::Create`); a new distinct file past 1024 gives `SyscallErrorTooManyFilesInSystem` (`ENFILE`), a failed growth `ENOMEM`, both before anything is created. `poll` takes up to the soft limit of entries in a heap buffer. Tests: `filesystem_tests.cpp` (a many-files FAT16 fixture, a test-only heap failure hook `tests/unit/support/heap_failure.h`, `Files::ResetDiskFileTable`), `filetest` opening 253 files.
    - *Step 2:* syscall 72 `ResourceLimit(resource, new, old)` (`ResourceLimitSyscall` in `process.cpp`, a `prlimit` on the caller: `RLIMIT_NOFILE` only, anything else `EINVAL`, a bad pointer `EFAULT`, then `Files::DescriptorLimits`: soft above hard `EINVAL`, hard above 1024 `EPERM`; lowering the soft limit closes nothing), libc `sys/resource.h` with `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` (`src/libc/resource.cpp`, a Swift `sys_resource` module), `OPEN_MAX` gone from `limits.h`, `sysconf(_SC_OPEN_MAX)` through `getrlimit`, and the shell's `ulimit -n [N]` and `-Hn [N]`. Tests: `syscall_tests.cpp` (dispatch), `libctest` (`CheckDescriptorLimits`, `CheckLimitInheritance` with a `libctest rlimit-child`, `CheckPollLimit`) and the shell part `limits.py` (`ulimit`) on both architectures.
- **Part 2: atomic rename onto an existing file (done).** A replacing `rename` is now one `Driver::Rename` call, so on ext2, ext3, ext4 and tmpfs the target name always exists after a crash and names the old file or the new one (ext3 and ext4 with no repair at all, ext2 possibly with a leaked inode that `e2fsck -fy` frees); FAT16 keeps its order, which can lose the target in a crash but never the source. Design `docs/design/M30-P2.md`.
    - *Step 1, the core:* no fix was needed, `Ext2Filesystem::RenameNode` already replaced in place and released the old inode last. `src/fs/core/tests/ext_rename_crash_tests.cpp` cuts every write of six replacements on ext3, ext4, ext4-64bit and (without a journal) ext2 variants, with `e2fsck` and `debugfs` after each cut; helpers moved to `tests/crash_helpers.{h,cpp}`; the write order is commented at `RenameNode` and FAT16's window at `Fat16Filesystem::Rename`.
    - *Step 2, the kernel:* `Files::Rename` makes every kind check first (`EISDIR`, `ENOTDIR`, `ENOTEMPTY` through `VFS::ReadDirectory`, `EBUSY` for a tracked target other than the source), then one `VFS::Rename`; it no longer calls `VFS::Remove` and its `EEXIST` fallback is gone (an error after the checks is the driver's own, `ENOENT` for a missing parent of the target). `CoreDriver::Rename` lost its `Exists` refusal. `Tmpfs::Rename` replaces a file, a link or an empty directory after all its checks (`HasChild`) and frees the target's node and bytes. A directory can replace an empty directory (it was `EEXIST`). An open target or source stays `EBUSY`. Tests: `filesystem_tests.cpp` (one core call: the target is old or new after every write on ext2, ext4 and FAT16, where the target may be missing but then the source holds the new data; `CheckReplacementRules` over ext2, ext3, ext4, FAT16 and tmpfs: the errno matrix, nothing written by a refusal, `EIO` and not `EEXIST` when writes fail), `virtual_files_tests.cpp` (tmpfs), `libctest` `CheckRename` on `/home` and `/tmp`, and the shell part `ext4.py` (`mv` onto an existing file on the ext4 root).
- **Part 3: `/sbin` for administrative programs (done).** `mount`, `umount`, `sync`, `net` and `dhcp` (`shutdown` and `reboot` are shell built-ins) move to `/sbin` (on the root, as `init` since M29 Part 3) and stay in `/system` as the rescue copies; the default `PATH` becomes `/bin:/sbin:/system`. `init.conf` and the tests name them by `PATH`. Users and permissions stay out of scope (`docs/ideas.md`, "Security"). (Done: `build.sh` moves `init`, `mount`, `umount`, `sync`, `net` and `dhcp` from `disk-programs/bin` to `disk-programs/sbin` (`shutdown` and `reboot` are shell built-ins and have no program), so the test disk's `/sbin` holds those six and `/bin` none of them; the initramfs's `/system` still has every program. `PATH` is `/bin:/sbin:/system` in `init.conf`, libc's `posix_spawnp` default, the shell's help and the `libctest` and `programs.py` expectations. `init.conf` and the tests already used bare names, so nothing else changed. The shell part `root_programs.py` checks `ls /sbin` lists the six and `ls /bin` none of them, that a program copied over `/sbin/mount` runs ahead of `/system/mount`, that deleting it falls back, and restores it.)
- **Part 4: services added beside the defaults (done).** `init` no longer reads `/etc/init.conf` instead of `/system/init.conf`; it merges the built-in services of `/system/init.conf`, one file per service group in `/etc/init.d/` (the administrator's), minus the built-in ones named in `/etc/init.disabled`, as launchd merges its directories; `env` lines merge the same way (a later one wins). A file that does not parse is skipped with a message, not the whole set. (Done: the merge is `src/user/init_config.{h,cpp}` (`Configuration`, `AddBuiltIn`, `DisableBuiltIns`, `AddServiceFile`, `SetVariable`, `ProgramName`; `MaximumServices` 8 to 16), host-tested in `tests/unit/kernel/process/init_config_tests.cpp`; `src/user/init.cpp` reads the files and prints. Decisions: `initramfs/init.conf` replaces `initramfs/etc/init.conf` (`build.sh` still installs it as `/system/init.conf`), so a first boot copies no `init.conf` into `/etc`; a leftover `/etc/init.conf` from M29 is not read and init prints `init: /etc/init.conf is not read any more; put services in /etc/init.d/`. A service's name is its program (first word of the command, directory dropped), a console's its terminal. Order: built-ins in file order, then `/etc/init.d/` files in name order (no dot files or directories, at most 16 files with names under 64 characters, 2047 bytes each). A service in `/etc/init.d/` replaces built-in services of the same name and takes its own place in the order; `/etc/init.d/` services never replace each other, but a console on a terminal another `/etc/init.d/` file holds skips that file. `/etc/init.disabled` lists one program name or terminal per line (comments allowed) and removes only built-in services, printing `init: /etc/init.disabled: not starting <command>`. An `/etc/init.d/` file with any bad line, too many services or variables, or a taken terminal is skipped whole with `init: <file>: ...` and nothing else changes; bad lines of the built-in file are skipped one by one as before. Env lines: a later `env NAME=value` replaces an earlier one (`SetVariable`). Tests: host tests for built-ins only, an added service, a disabled built-in (by program, terminal and not an `/etc/init.d/` one), a replacing file, env order, skipped files and limits; `STORAGE` boot 1 creates `/etc/init.d/storage` (a `once grep` that prints a marker) and `/etc/init.disabled` (`dhcp`) in `CheckEtc`, boot 2 finds them unchanged, the harness checks the marker and the `not starting dhcp` line on both boots and reads the files with `debugfs` (`VerifyRootEtc`); `ROOT_TEST` on `vda1` adds a broken and a good `/etc/init.d` file with `debugfs` and expects the skip message and the good service's marker, and no marker or skip message on the ext3 and ext2 roots. Follow-up: when the merged set has no console service, `EnsureConsole` adds `console /dev/tty1 shell` with `init: no console service; starting a shell on /dev/tty1`; host-tested, and the empty `vda1` root's `/etc/init.disabled` names all four terminals so `ROOT_TEST` expects the message and a working shell.)
- **Part 5: several volumes of one filesystem type at once (done).** Each type has one driver instance (`Drivers::Ext4()` and the rest), so with an ext4 root no second ext4 volume can be mounted. Drivers become per-volume objects created when a partition is found, registered by partition in `VFS::TypeRegistry` (`drivers.cpp`, `type_registry.cpp`, `mount_table.cpp`, `/proc/mounts`), and the "one instance per filesystem" limit goes. Tests: two ext4 volumes on the test disk mounted at once, written and checked by `e2fsck -fn`. Design `docs/design/M30-P5.md` (with MBR extended partitions, Linux numbering and per-volume locking, as the user chose). Steps: 1 MBR extended partitions and `Range::number` in the core; 2 one driver per partition; 3 the test disk (166 MiB, data ext4 `vda5`, ext2 renamed `vda6`) and QEMU two-ext4 checks; 4 lock infrastructure and `unit-test-tsan`; 5 per-volume locking in `Files` and the VFS.
    - *(Step 1 done: `Partition::Find` walks the first MBR extended entry's EBR chain (`FindLogicals` in `src/fs/core/partition/partition.cpp`: at most 64 records, each strictly after the last and inside the container, a logical after its record and inside the container and disk, a bad link or record ends the chain) and `Partition::Range::number` is Linux's number (primary slot, logicals from 5, GPT index plus one); `CoreDriver::Mount` takes `partition_number` from it and ignores numbers above 16, `fscli`'s `disk.img:N` and `info` and `kernel_test.cpp`'s `PartitionNeedsRecovery` use it instead of the list position. On today's test disk numbers equal positions. A logical of any type takes a number, as in Linux, so an unsupported or out-of-range logical leaves a gap. Tests: `partition_tests.cpp` (`WriteEbr` in `partition_image.cpp`; the test disk's layout, relative offsets, loops with counted reads, bad logicals and links, numbering and capacity, one extended entry, GPT and 4096-byte sectors; the `sfdisk -J` comparison skips without `sfdisk`) and `filesystem_tests.cpp` (a FAT16 in a logical is `vda5`).)*
    - *(Step 2 done: a static pool of 16 `CoreDriver`s, one per partition number (`Drivers::Volume(n)`, `VolumeOf`, `FindPartitions`, `OpenVolume`, `ForgetVolume` in `drivers.cpp`), replaces the per-type singletons and `Drivers::Disk/Ext2/Ext3/Ext4/Mount*`. `CoreDriver::OpenPartition` opens a partition read-only as the first of `Kinds[]` (FAT16, ext2, ext3, ext4) whose probe and mount accept it, a passing probe with a failing mount falling through, and the options and `TypeName()` follow that kind; `Forget` closes it. `VFS::MountBlockDevice` opens every partition, keeps a volume mounted at a non-root point, forgets vanished slots, picks the root over the pool (`root=vda5` and the like work) and re-registers each slot with `Unregister` then `Register`; `TypeRegistry::Capacity` is 32 (static_assert: built-ins plus 16 volumes) and `Register` no longer renames a driver's source. A volume mounts at one point at most (`EBUSY`). Host tests in `filesystem_tests.cpp` mount two ext2, ext3 and ext4 volumes at once (own files, `st_dev`, writes inside the own partition, `EBUSY`/`ENOENT`), keep their journals apart, tell the same inode number on two volumes apart, give `EXDEV`, fail writes in one volume only, use an ext4 root beside a second ext4, rescan, fill 16 partitions (GPT, 17 entries) and follow the options per kind; `mount_table_tests.cpp` fills the registry. The test disk and QEMU behaviour are unchanged. Mutation checks: a shared `core_read_only`, a shared `Partition::Volume`, a `FileKey` comparing only the node and a registry capacity of 16 or no `Unregister` each fail the tests above; a shared `last_error` is only visible with concurrent tasks and is left to step 5.)*
    - *(Step 3 done: the test disk is 166 MiB (`tools/create_test_disk.py`): the MBR's slot 4 is an extended container (type 0x05, 148 to 162 MiB) of two logicals one MiB after their EBRs, the data ext4 `vda5` (4 MiB, label `FEATHERDATA`, `make_data_volume`, `/hello.txt` and `/samples/hello.txt` = "data volume") and ext2 as `vda6` (8 MiB, moved); GPT holds the same volumes at the same sectors, entry 4 empty, entries 5 and 6; the free 4 MiB tail stays clear of the backup table (asserted) and `sfdisk -J` is compared when installed (not run here: `sfdisk` is absent on macOS). `EXT3_END`, `DATA_START`/`DATA_END` and the container constants are exported; `storage.py`, `root.py` and `power.py` import them. ext2 is `vda6` in `kernel_test.cpp`, `storage.py`, `power.py`, `shell/ext2.py`, `root.py` and the matrix's `ext2_root` (`KERNEL_ROOT=vda6`). `CheckSecondExt4` (after `CheckExt3AndExt2`) mounts `ext4 vda5` at `/data` beside the root, checks both volumes' files, runs the `/os` writes on it (boot 2 first reads the `/periodic.txt` the first boot's shell left in its journal), writes `/data/boot<N>.bin` and `/boot<N>-root.txt`, expects `Busy` for a second mount of `vda5` or `vda1` and `EXDEV` for a rename or link across, unmounts and prints `KERN/STORAGE: two ext4 volumes mounted at once; boot N`. Design `docs/design/M30-P6.md` (the page cache replaces the core's `BlockCache` in the kernel, a quarter of the free frames at boot, `Cached:` in `/proc/meminfo`, as the user chose). Steps: 1 the page cache in the core (`PageCache`, `CachedDevice`, `PageSource`); 2 the core's metadata path over it (`CachesBlocks`, a pass-through `BlockCache`, crash identity and the kernel-configuration test build); 3 kernel frames, sizing, reclaim and device counters; 4 `CoreDriver` integration, overlap and `/proc/meminfo`; 5 QEMU checks and docs.
    - *(Step 1 done: the page cache in the core, nothing else changed (the kernel, `BlockCache` and ext do not use it yet). `src/fs/core/include/fs/page_cache.h` and `common/page_cache.cpp`: `PageSource` (`AllocatePage`, `FreePage`, `Headroom`, the host's memory policy), `PageCache` (`Initialize(source, limit)` allocates the entry array and hash buckets once, `Shrink(n)`, `Balance()` which shrinks one page at a time while `Headroom()` is 0, `InvalidateAll()`, `Statistics()`) and `CachedDevice`, a `BlockDevice` decorator (`Attach(cache, lower)`, `Invalidate`, `Detach`). Pages are 4 KiB counted from the volume's sector 0 (the last one short, nothing outside the volume read), keyed by (volume id, page) with ids that are never reused, in one LRU list under one `Host::Lock`; the lock covers index, list, counter and one-page copy work and is never held across a lower call, `AllocatePage`, `FreePage` or a log. A miss takes a frame (a new one while `pages < limit` and `Headroom() > 0`, else the LRU tail's), reads the whole page, copies the asked sectors out and inserts only if its volume's `epoch` and the cache's `generation` (bumped by `InvalidateAll`) have not changed since the miss; `Write` bumps the epoch, calls `lower.Write`, bumps it again, then copies the bytes into cached pages (success) or unlinks them (failure); `Flush` is `lower.Flush()`. A page whose fill fails is retried as just the asked sectors uncached (`bypasses`), so the cache never reports an error the device would not, and a cache with no frame reads uncached; a null cache or a sector size that does not divide 4096 passes through, and a request that is not whole sectors or leaves the volume goes to the lower device unchanged. `PageCacheStatistics` counts hits, misses, bypasses, evictions, reclaimed and invalidated pages. Differences from the design: `Entry` has no `sectors` field (every hit lies inside the volume, so the page length is recomputed from the volume), and `Balance` frees one page per step instead of a batch so it stops as soon as there is headroom. Tests (`tests/page_cache_tests.cpp`, T1-T13: hit, short last page and sentinels, an LRU model, a random model over three volumes at 512 and 2048 byte sectors, write-through against a recording device, failed writes, read errors, eviction, per-volume invalidation, the fill epoch with `MemoryDevice::on_read`, keys and re-attach, accounting with `TestPageSource`, `Balance`, pass-through sizes, and a `[concurrency]` test of four volumes plus a shrinking thread that also runs under `./build.sh unit-test-tsan`, now building and running `featherdisk_core_tests` as well); `MemoryDevice` gained `read_count`, `read_sectors` and `on_read`. Mutation checks: no epoch check fails T10, skipping `Flush` or a lower write or writing twice fails T5, caching a failed fill or returning a page-wide error fails T7.)*

A 0-slot `BlockCache` (`common/block_cache.cpp`, `SlotCount()`) is a pass-through: it still checks the block size, allocates no data and creates no lock, `Read` is one `device->Read` of the whole block into `scratch` and a copy of the asked part (none when `out` is that part of `scratch`), `ReadUncached` is unchanged and `Write` is the one `device->Write`. Initialising with 0 used to clamp to one slot, so 0 now means pass-through. `Ext2Filesystem::MetadataCacheSlots()` is the test accessor. Tests: T26 in `block_cache_tests.cpp` (one device read of the whole block per `Read` even when repeated, one write with the same arguments and its error, `ReadUncached`, sizes still refused); `page_cache_metadata_tests.cpp` has T27 (a `RecordingDevice` between the ext mount and an attached `CachedDevice` sees a read for every `GetAttributes` and `Lookup` on ext4, ext2 and ext3 while the `MemoryDevice` below sees none after the first; with no cache attached it sees no repeats), the 0-slot or 32 KiB check over attached, detached and plain devices, and T15 (random ext4 and ext2 workloads through a cache of 8 pages: e2fsck clean and the image, flush marks and write digest equal to the uncached run). Scenarios: the mixed 26-change scenario on ext3, ext4, ext4-64bit (every write) and ext2 `1k`, the replacing renames on the same three and on `1k` and `ext4-nojournal`, the five split changes on `ext3-groups` and `ext4`, and the log wrap on ext3 and ext4. On a volume without a journal the stopped image is a copy (the mount ends on the device once writes work again), so the loss patterns apply only to journalled volumes. To share them, the scenarios moved to `tests/crash_scenarios.{h,cpp}` (`MixedScenario`, `CycleScenario`, `RandomScenario`, the split and rename scenarios and fixtures), `FirstStepWhere` to `crash_helpers`, and `RunReference`/`RunCrashed` take a `page_cache_pages` argument (`RunReference` also accepts a volume without a journal); `TestPageSource` and `RecordingDevice` moved to `page_cache_support`. The `PageCache` lives in raw aligned storage constructed by `InitializeCache`, since the kernel runs no static destructors (`__cxa_atexit` is not linked). `src/kernel/storage/core_block_device.{h,cpp}`: `DeviceStatistics()` returns the sectors read and written and the flushes counted in `VirtioDisk`, below any cache. Departures from the design: the sizing code is its own file; the periodic trim is not `PageCache::Balance()` (which stops at the first page of headroom) but `TrimToMargin`, which starts when the headroom is 0 and goes on one page at a time until it is half the watermark, so the cache does not regrow straight into the watermark; `CacheLimit` caps at the part above the watermark. Tests (`tests/unit/kernel/storage/page_cache_tests.cpp`, tags `[page-cache]`): T16 `Watermark` and `CacheLimit` on sample machines, `PageCache::Balance` and `TrimToMargin` over a counting fake source (exact page counts, nothing below headroom, an empty cache ends it); T17 over the real `page_allocator.cpp`, now host-built on a 16 MiB buffer standing in for physical memory (a memory map and an HHDM offset pointing at it): one reclaimer call with 32 asked and the allocator lock free (a flag checks it), one retry, no call while a frame is free or for a contiguous request, none with a null reclaimer, no frame lost; and the kernel's own `InitializeCache`, `ReclaimFrames` and `BalanceCache` on that allocator (exhausting the allocator empties the cache and takes every frame; the balance leaves exactly watermark plus margin free). New in `filesystem_tests.cpp` (tag `[page-cache]`, the oracle the host disk's `read_sector_count` and `write_sector_count`): T18 (a file on FAT16, ext2 and ext4 read twice and `stat`ed twice with no sector read, and after an invalidation a `stat` reads again, so nothing below the page cache keeps metadata), T19 (an overwrite reaches the host disk before it returns, the next read hits, and `e2fsck -fn` is clean on the unmounted ext4 partition when e2fsck is installed), T20 (a moved partition reads its new bytes, a vanished partition's pages are freed by the rescan, an unmount frees the volume's pages, a kept mount keeps them and two volumes read their own bytes), T21 (two MBR entries over one FAT16 range: the second volume's repeated read goes to the disk, the first's does not, a write through the first is seen by the second), T22 (`Cached:` in `/proc/meminfo` is 4 kB per page, 0 kB for an empty cache); the shell part `files.py` now expects `Cached: `. Known gap: when two open volumes overlap, a write through the later (uncached) one is not seen by pages the earlier one already cached, since only the earlier one has a cache to update; the design's rule only keeps both from caching. **Complete when:** on both targets, a process can hold more than 32 descriptors, a crash during a replacing rename on ext3/ext4 never loses the target, the administrative programs run from `/sbin`, services in `/etc/init.d/` start beside the built-in ones and can disable them across reboots, two ext4 volumes are mounted at once, repeated reads hit the page cache, and `e2fsck -fn` finds no errors after each case.

### 31. FeatherFS And The Feather Disk Split (Planned, Not Started)

Deferred by the user after M29; to be planned in detail when it starts.

- **FeatherFS:** a filesystem of our own design with journaling, so files survive a crash in the middle of a write, in the shared core beside FAT16 and ext.
- **The Feather Disk split:** `src/fs/core/` (namespace `FeatherDisk`, MIT OR Apache-2.0) moves to its own repository by a subtree split, for the planned Mac and Windows ext app, and FeatherOS takes it back as a dependency.

## Architecture Boundaries

- Keep framebuffer terminal, logging API, panic/assert API, VFS, syscall definitions, and scheduler policy architecture-neutral when possible.
- Keep port I/O, UART drivers, exception entry, interrupt controllers, page-table manipulation, context switching, and syscall entry under the matching `arch` backend.
- Do not share x86 GDT/TSS/IDT structures with AArch64; AArch64 uses exception levels, vector tables, and the GIC instead.
- Avoid fixed physical addresses. Obtain usable memory from Limine's memory map and use explicit 64-bit address types.
- Keep early logging independent of dynamic allocation so it remains usable during allocator failures.
